# BOB Stock App — Feature and Decision Register

> **GENERATED FILE — DO NOT EDIT.** Edit `register/features.json` / `register/decisions.json`
> and run `node register/build.js`. `test/check-register.js` fails if this file is hand-edited,
> if a code reference in it has gone stale, or if a named test no longer exists.

*Generated 2026-08-02. 428 features across 13 areas, 364 decisions.*

## Read this first

Every feature carries **four separate statuses**, because on this project they genuinely
come apart and blurring them has cost real time:

| Status | Question it answers |
|---|---|
| **built** | Does the code exist? |
| **proven** | Does a test cover it? (a gate you have never seen fail is not a gate) |
| **cloud** | Is the server side deployed? |
| **on** | Does it actually run for a real person today? |

A feature can be built, proven, deployed **and still switched off**. Right now 144 features are exactly that. "We built it" and "it works for staff" are different sentences.

**Today: 221 features live, 13 partly on, 194 not running.**

<details><summary>Notes and gotchas (32)</summary>

- **Log in with a username and password** — On the LIVE site the password check is 100% in the browser. Anyone who can open the browser's developer tools can bypass it. That is a known, accepted limit for the trusted internal alpha (CLAUDE.md §6, framework rule P-13) — it is not security, it is a convenience gate. There is no 'forgot password' and no self-service reset: a Director resets it in User Management.
- **Cloud-checked login (each account verified on the server)** — THE BIG ONE. The Azure Function is deployed and the `bob-stock-user-verify-staging` Logic App exists, but the address of that Logic App was deliberately never put into the app's cloud settings (AZURE-CHUNK9-WAVE-REVIEW.md line 44 calls it DORMANT), AND the settings channel itself has never worked (sync.js:64 still ships the placeholder `%%CONFIG_URL%%`, bail-out at sync.js:691). So `Sync._userVerifyUrl` is always null on a real device and this whole path is skipped. Nobody has ever logged in through the server.
- **Logging in with no internet (device-local password check)** — Chicken-and-egg: the offline copy is only created by a successful ONLINE login, and online login never runs (see above), so no device has one. Two consequences worth knowing when this does switch on: (a) revoking someone only bites when they are online — a deactivated account with a cached offline copy can still sign in on that device until it reconnects; (b) 210,000 scrambling rounds means the offline sign-in takes roughly a quarter-second, deliberately.
- **Old-style fallback login (first-run migration window)** — This is the login path every real user will actually hit right now, on both `main` and the branch, and it is the least covered by tests. There is NO limit on wrong password attempts in the browser — the 5-strikes lockout lives only in the cloud Logic App.
- **Staying signed in (and being signed out when the tab closes)** — There is no 'remember me'. On a shared store PC that is the right behaviour; on a phone it means signing in every session. The ticket is matched against a copy held in the device database, so signing out on one tab invalidates the ticket for other tabs of the same account.
- **Sign Out (and it clears half-finished work)** — Signing out also wipes every in-memory cloud credential (session ticket, PIN grant, action approvals) and the 24-hour stock-take unlock.
- **Screen lock after 10 minutes idle, unlocked with a 4-digit PIN** — Two gotchas. (1) The lock screen has NO 'sign out' or escape — if the PIN is wrong or missing, the only way out is to close the tab. (2) On the working branch, accounts created through the cloud path get no local PIN at all (index.html:3714), so those users would hit 'No PIN set. Contact admin.' and be stuck behind an un-dismissable overlay. Unlimited PIN guesses, no lockout, 4 digits.
- **Locking the screen also throws away your cloud credentials** — The lock and the clearing both run today; there is simply nothing to clear, because no cloud passes are ever issued (see cloud login). Side effect once it is on: after an idle lock, staff must re-enter the 24-hour stock-take PIN, not just the screen PIN.
- **The six job types (roles)** — There is NO seeded Territory manager account — the role exists everywhere in the code and menus but nobody has ever been one. `Auth.isAtLeast()` at index.html:943 ranks franchisee BELOW store manager, which is wrong for this business; the code comment at :994 warns never to use it and nothing does, but it is a live trap for the next engineer. `Auth.canSeeStore()` at :984 is written and never called anywhere.
- **The permission list (who can do what)** — BROWSER-ONLY today. It stops honest mistakes and catches regressions; it does not stop a determined person with developer tools. The five extra entries on the branch are the view controls: see cost prices, see selling prices, see older/archived data, see store-comparison charts, edit the access policy. One gate is still hard-coded outside the list: the transfer hub's 'New Transfer' button checks a fixed role list at phase2.js:1208, so changing the policy would not change that button.
- **Cloud-published Account Access policy (overrides the built-in list)** — BUILT AND OFF. The Function is deployed and the write Logic App exists and was proven to refuse an unauthorised device (401), but no policy has ever been published and the settings channel that would deliver it has never worked. With no policy adopted, the app falls back to the built-in list, which is exactly today's behaviour. Publishing is the activation moment and it CHANGES BEHAVIOUR (see the staff-PIN-to-receive entry).
- **Per-account exceptions ('always allow' / 'always block' one person)** — Exceptions are keyed by USERNAME on both sides (fix AA-01) — an exception written against an internal id is silently ignored, which is proven deliberately.
- **Account Access screen (Settings → Account Access)** — The tab only appears for someone holding 'edit this access policy' (Directors). Pressing Publish always asks for the password, and the server re-validates everything — the screen is an editor, never the gate. Publishing today returns 'Access-policy endpoint not configured yet (server phase pending)'.
- **Server-side permission decision (the real gate)** — Deployed as live routes on the `bob-stock-money-fn` Function App. BUT the Logic Apps that would CALL it for most operations are not applied yet — AZURE-CHUNK-AA-STAGING-LEDGER.md lists items 5-11 (movement/step ingest checks, cost reads, archive reads, user management, catalogue writes, the PIN grant route) as still outstanding. So the decision engine exists and works; almost nothing routes through it yet.
- **Server-side policy validation and safety floors** — The two locked settings are 'Edit access policy' and 'User management' — those always require the password and the screen renders them with a padlock. Known tracked defect SRV-P3: the write Logic App saves three items one after another, so a mid-way failure can temporarily block the Director from re-publishing until it is reconciled. It fails safe (no bad policy ships).
- **Password re-prompt before dangerous actions (sudo)** — Completely invisible today: with cloud auth dormant, _sudoPrompt returns a placeholder and shows no dialog, so every one of those actions proceeds exactly as it does now. The prompt always targets the person actually at the keyboard, not whoever the last session belonged to.
- **The 24-hour Stock Take PIN** — The LOCAL version (PIN scrambled and stored on the device) is switched on and works today. The CLOUD version — where the PIN lives only on the server, is checked online, and issues a signed unlock pass that the server can verify on every stock-take row — is built and proven but off, and item 10 of the staging ledger (the Logic App route that mints the pass) is not applied. Note the cloud version needs internet at the moment the PIN is typed; Kunal accepted that trade-off.
- **Clearing the PIN instantly kills every live unlock** — AA-20 is DECIDED, not open: "Clear PIN" instantly revokes already-granted access. RESOLVED: the deployed code implements instant revocation — grants carry a pinEpoch and clearing or changing the PIN bumps it, so every outstanding grant dies immediately (azure-functions/src/functions/accessPolicy.js:103-117; the app drops its local grant at sync.js:441-446). Decided by Kunal 2026-07-10 and recorded in AZURE-CHUNK-AA-LA-CHANGES.md.
- **Store account needs the PIN to receive a Head Office transfer** — THE ONE DELIBERATE BEHAVIOUR CHANGE hiding inside activation. Today staff receive transfers freely. The Account Access screen warns about it in the activation banner. Nothing else about publishing changes day-to-day behaviour.
- **Which menu each role sees** — Menus are cosmetic only — hiding a link does not stop someone typing the page name. The real protection is the guard at the top of each page. Every signed-in person can reach the Transfers Hub (so anyone can receive), but 'New Transfer' only appears for franchisee and above.
- **Page-level guards (typing a page name doesn't get you in)** — The router does not gate anything — each page gates itself, so a page that forgets is silently open. That is exactly the bug fixed on the Store Comparison page (AA-02) and it is why the guard pattern matters.
- **Store scoping — which shops a person can see** — Browser-only filtering today: a live device still HOLDS every store's data, it just doesn't show it. Gap worth knowing: a Territory manager is not treated as 'store level' at index.html:2778, so Stock Overview shows them ALL stores regardless of which stores they were assigned. Nobody is a Territory manager today, so it has never bitten.
- **Server-enforced store isolation (Chunk 10)** — Converged and heavily tested, but it depends on the cloud echoing back the allowed store list, which requires the server-phase Logic Apps and the (dead) settings channel. Until then, isolation is browser-only.
- **User Management screen (add / edit / deactivate accounts)** — Guardrails that work today: you cannot delete your own account, cannot delete or demote the last Director, cannot reuse a username, and the PIN must be exactly 4 digits — all checked BEFORE anything is changed. On the live version accounts are created purely on that one device, so a new account must be created on every device or arrive by backup restore.
- **Cloud-managed accounts, lockout and Unlock** — The `bob-stock-user-admin-staging` Logic App exists and was driven end-to-end against the real cloud during Chunk 9. It is dormant because its address is not published to devices. Passwords are set by the Director only — minimum 8 characters, 10 for a Director, enforced on the server (validateUser.js:157-166). The extra permission check on this Logic App (staging ledger item 8) is not applied.
- **Login Audit — who signed in, on what device** — Easy to over-read. It is a per-device diary held in that browser, capped at 500 entries; it is never sent anywhere and never combined across devices. It also cannot see failed attempts. The page says so honestly.
- **Audit Log — deleted movements with reason and author** — Staff cannot delete at all — they get a 5-minute Undo instead. Store managers and above can delete, which is why the log exists.
- **Passwords, PINs and passes are kept out of backups** — IMPORTANT DIFFERENCE between live and branch. On the LIVE site the deny-list (main index.html:3295) does NOT include password or pin, so hashes still travel in a backup and a restore keeps everyone able to log in. On the branch they ARE stripped — which means restoring a backup there leaves every account with no password and no screen-lock PIN, and anyone without a cached offline copy cannot sign back in. Also note the code comment at index.html:3886 still claims hashes are 'intentionally kept'; the code disagrees and the code wins.
- **Price and cost visibility controls** — Only 'see cost prices' and 'see older data' have real cloud teeth (the server refuses to send the data). 'See selling prices' is honestly labelled a screen-only hint — the price list is public catalogue data sent to every device. A fifth switch, 'see charts', was deliberately DROPPED because it could not be enforced anywhere clean and a switch that lies is worse than no switch.
- **Automatic clean-up when someone loses access** — It also runs on the normal morning start-up when nobody is signed in yet — in that case it clears the cost data anyway rather than guess, and an entitled device simply re-downloads it.
- **Cross-tab pass relay** — Without this, a row approved in a background tab would be pushed with no approval attached and permanently quarantined by the server once ingest checking is switched on.
- **Device keys as the first door (Sync Keys screen)** — Belongs mostly to the device-authentication area, listed here because it is the outer layer of login: device first, then person. Staging keys were regenerated on 22 July 2026 and every one verified against the deployed checker. Never enter or share a key value in writing.

</details>

Lines are never edited and never overwritten — like a bank statement. When the app needs to show you 'we have 14 of these at Booragoon', it adds up every IN and subtracts every OUT for that product at that store, right there and then. | **The movement classifier — direction (this is what moves the stock)** | Every movement line has a type, and the classifier turns that type into one word: in, out, or none. 'In' adds to the shelf count, 'out' subtracts, 'none' means the line is ignored (a deleted line, or a type the app doesn't recognise). Nine types exist: in, return_in, transfer_in and adjustment_in all add; out, move_out, transfer_out, wastage and adjustment_out all subtract; deleted is ignored. I also ran the classifier standalone today against a 15-case table and every case matched. |
| **The movement classifier — category (this is what the reports use)** | Direction only tells you whether stock went up or down. Category tells you WHAT actually happened, and it works it out from where the stock went. The same 'out' movement is a sale, wastage, a transfer or in-house use depending on the destination you picked at step 3. The destination always wins — a wastage-sounding reason on a transfer does not turn it into wastage. And if the destination is missing or 'Other', the line lands in an 'unclassified' bucket rather than being silently counted as a sale. | director, head office, franchisee (through every report) | LIVE | `index.html:1114-1142 (category) · index.html:1143-1145 (isSale / isConsumption / isWastage)` | test/smoke-test.js S-92 (per-category labels, destination authoritative, and stock maths unchanged), S-94 (wastage report excludes transfers carrying a wastage reason), S-101 (labels with a store name suffixed still classify correctly) |

If Head Office has filed away old history, the app starts from the filed-away opening balance and only replays the movements since. <details><summary>Notes and gotchas (31)</summary>

- **Log Movement wizard (the 4-step form)** — No permission check at all on logging — every logged-in role can log a movement. The only per-role difference is that staff do not see the RETURN IN button (index.html:1922). Confirmed present on the live site's branch (main).
- **Location picker (which store am I logging for?)** — The dropdown lists only ACTIVE stores, and hides warehouses unless they are a Franchise Office. Ardross and Bunbury were DELETED from the seed on 2026-08-02 (33 practice movements, 12 threshold entries and both store rows removed); test/check-seed-integrity.js now fails if either reappears.
- **Step 1 — Stock IN / Stock OUT / Return In** — Underneath, these are the movement 'types' in/out/return_in. Six more types exist that this screen never creates — see the classifier entry.
- **Step 2 — find the product (type chips, category chips, search)** — Only products marked active appear. Search matches the product name OR its reference code.
- **Step 3 — quantity, with a whole-number guard** — The screen also shows the current stock on hand at that location, floored at zero so staff never see a negative number (index.html:1947).
- **Step 3 — 'Stock Coming From' (for INs and Returns)** — Stock OUT has NO 'coming from' — the source is always the location you selected at the top. Verified by running the classifier: 'HO Warehouse', 'Another Store', 'Franchise Office' all classify a Stock IN as a transfer; 'Supplier', 'Other' or blank classify it as a delivery.
- **Step 3 — 'Stock Going To' (for OUTs)** — If someone picks 'Other', the movement lands in an 'unclassified' bucket — deliberately, so it is never silently counted as a sale.
- **Which store? — the second dropdown for transfers** — GAP, verified by running the option lists: 'Franchise Office' is offered as a Stock IN source but is NOT in the picker list, so the app never asks WHICH franchise office — the counterparty is left blank. 'HO Warehouse' and 'Supplier' also get no picker (fine — they are unambiguous). Also, the store list offered excludes the Head Office warehouse entirely, so you can never name Head Office as the specific source store.
- **Reason dropdown that changes with your answer** — Dead config: REASONS_MAP.in has a 'Franchise Store' entry (index.html:1826) that can never be reached, because 'Franchise Store' is not offered as a Stock IN source. Harmless, but it is a leftover.
- **Return Condition (Returns only)** — IMPORTANT: the condition is recorded but changes NOTHING. A return marked 'Damaged — Write Off' still ADDS a unit back to the shelf count, and does not appear in the wastage report. Someone must log a separate Stock Out to Wastage/Damage to take it back off. Grep-verified: returnCondition is referenced only in the wizard and in the display, nowhere in the stock maths or reports.
- **'Your Name' on every movement** — It is FREE TEXT — anyone can type anyone's name. It is not the logged-in account. (For deletions the app does record the verified account separately — see the Delete entry.)
- **Franchise Transfer Pricing banner on the Confirm screen** — TWO problems. (1) The variable is called destStore but it is actually the location selected at the top of the page — so if Head Office selects 'Cockburn' and logs a normal Customer Sale, this 'Franchise Transfer Pricing' banner still appears. Misleading. (2) It reads the flat franchiseDiscount number off the product or the store, NOT the dated era-aware Pricing lens, so the figure it displays can differ from what the invoice would actually bill. It also cannot express Kunal's decided model (universal rate → product-type rate → per-product rate) because the by-type tier does not exist.
- **'Not enough stock' block on a Stock Out** — Only applies to OUT movements. There is no equivalent ceiling on Stock In or Returns (correct — those add). If a product is already sitting at a negative number, every Out will be blocked until it is corrected by a stock take.
- **Double-tap protection on Confirm** — Found in a blind audit (Wave G) after it happened for real in testing.
- **THE APPEND-ONLY LEDGER — pressing Confirm writes one line, forever** — The write is DURABLE — if the device's storage refuses it, the movement is rolled back and the user gets a hard 'could not be saved' error rather than a false success. It also works with no internet: the line is saved locally and would sync later.
- **ONE-SIDED movements — a transfer logged here only moves one end** — Not a bug — it is the design. But it is the single easiest way for staff to make the numbers wrong, and there is no warning on screen telling them the other store still has to receive it.
- **Sale price frozen at the moment of logging** — The in-code comment at index.html:2191-2193 says this is 'local-only, NOT pushed to SharePoint yet'. THE COMMENT IS STALE — sync.js:1309 does send UnitPriceAtTime, and the staging cloud has the column (AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-STAGING-LEDGER.md:25). But that newer sync.js is only on the working branch; the live site's sync.js does NOT send it.
- **Franchise price stamps when Head Office supplies a franchise directly** — SIGNIFICANT: this code can never fire from the Log Movement wizard. It requires the movement's source-store-id to be exactly 'head_office', but the wizard only sets a source-store-id when the user picks 'Another Store' or 'Franchise Store', and the store list offered there deliberately excludes the Head Office warehouse (index.html:2076). So a franchise logging 'Stock In from HO Warehouse' gets NO price stamp — yet it still lands on the franchise invoice via the text fallback at index.html:4876. Not on the live site at all (main has no _pricingSubmitGate).
- **Today's Movements list (under the wizard)** — 'Today' is the Perth calendar date, not UTC — proven by S-19. Only the selected location's movements appear.
- **Undo within 5 minutes** — Undo does not silently erase the line: it writes a 'tombstone' — a cancelling marker that other devices can see, so the movement disappears everywhere rather than only on this device.
- **Delete a movement (managers and above, with a reason)** — The permission check is done twice — on the button and again on the actual delete — so it cannot be bypassed by poking at the page. The audit row records the typed name AND the real logged-in account.
- **Archived movements can no longer be undone or deleted** — Server side (the archive Logic App + the published balance snapshot) is applied and proven on the STAGING cloud only. The client half is not on the live site — main has no _coveredBySnapshot. And the snapshot arrives via the config channel, which has never worked (sync.js:691), so no device has ever received one.
- **The movement classifier — direction (this is what moves the stock)** — 'move_out' is a dead type — nothing in the app creates it any more (only the classifier, a legacy reversal rule at db.js:928, and one sentinel still mention it). Harmless, but it exists purely for old data.
- **The movement classifier — category (this is what the reports use)** — Added in Wave K after a blind audit found wastage and transfers were all being counted as SALES. Deliberately forward-only: old lines with no destination stay 'unclassified' rather than being retro-guessed. Two separate functions on purpose — changing category can never change a stock number.
- **The quantity cache (why it is instant)** — A deliberate rule at db.js:1145: deletes NEVER try to reverse a nudge, they always force a full replay — reversing was the source of an old bug.
- **Cache-integrity health check** — BUILT AND PROVEN BUT NEVER RUNS. Grep-verified: the only callers anywhere are the two sentinels in test/smoke-test.js. Nothing in the app calls it — not at start-up, not on a schedule, and there is no Director button for it. Its comment even says 'call from console to verify integrity'. This is the app's own safety net for stock drift, and it is switched off.
- **Low-stock warning right after logging** — Only fires if a Director has actually set a minimum for that product/store. No thresholds set = no warning ever.
- **Half-finished forms are wiped on logout** — Found in a blind audit (Wave G).
- **Sending a logged movement to the cloud** — NOTHING HAS EVER SYNCED. sync.js still ships CONFIG_URL: '%%CONFIG_URL%%' — a build placeholder no step ever filled in — on BOTH the working branch (sync.js:64) and the live branch, verified today. _fetchRemoteConfig bails at sync.js:691, so the app never learns the push address. On the live site it is worse: main's sync.js does not even contain StockFrom / StockTo / UnitPriceAtTime, so the sale-vs-wastage labels and the frozen price would not be sent even if the channel worked. Today, a logged movement lives on exactly one browser on one device.
- **Server-side tamper seal on a movement row (EconSig)** — Applied and proven on the STAGING cloud (2026-07-23), not on live. And since nothing syncs at all, no real movement has ever been sealed.
- **Store-isolation guard at the moment of writing** — Working branch only — grep-verified that main's db.js has no _scopeGuardOnWrite at all.

</details>

<details><summary>Notes and gotchas (33)</summary>

- **Stock Take counting sheet** — Pure client screen. It is on the live 'main' branch, so it is the version a user would get today. The app has never actually been used, so no real count has ever been entered. Every product with active=true is listed, whether or not it has stock, so the untrimmed list is long — the filters below are how you make it manageable.
- **Choose which store you are counting** — IMPORTANT GAP: the store list is d.stores.filter(s=>s.active && (s.type!=='warehouse' \|\| s.isFranchiseOffice)) — so the Head Office warehouse (type 'warehouse', not a franchise office) is EXCLUDED. Nobody, not even a director, can run a stock take on the HO warehouse from this screen. The Cockburn Franchise Office IS included because it is flagged isFranchiseOffice. Also note the store list is drawn from the seed, which still lists 11 stores including the closed Ardross and Bunbury.
- **Count only part of the shop (Product Type and Category filters)** — There is NO concept of a 'full' stock take in the code — no completeness check, no 'you missed 40 products' warning, no all-products-required mode. A take records only the products where a number was typed. Numbers you have already typed survive switching filters (they are held in Pages._stData keyed by product), but they are wiped if you change store or leave the page.
- **Live difference badge as you type** — Cosmetic only — nothing is saved until Submit.
- **Counts must be whole numbers, no negatives** — The input box also carries min="0", but that is only a browser hint — the real gate is safeInt at submit.
- **Submit a clean stock take (everything matched)** — The record is saved with status 'clean' and is then effectively invisible — see the 'no stock take history list' gap. Clean takes never appear in Discrepancy History (that report only shows approved takes with differences).
- **Explain the differences before submitting** — The reason list is hard-coded in the source — head office cannot add or rename reasons without a code change. The note field is capped at 200 characters.
- **Submit for director approval (stock unchanged)** — Guarded against a double-tap by the _stSubmitting flag (index.html:2538). The saboteur harness attacks this (test/saboteur-runner.js:323).
- **Pending Stock-Take Approvals panel (director)** — BIG PRACTICAL PROBLEM: directors and head office have NO 'Stock Take' link in their sidebar (index.html:6025-6078) and no 'Take' button in the phone bottom bar (index.html:6145-6161). The only way a director reaches this panel is the 'Stock Take' quick-action button on the Dashboard (index.html:4504). There is no badge, no count, no alert anywhere telling a director that approvals are waiting.
- **Review a pending count** — Read-only view — the director cannot edit a counted figure or a reason, only accept or reject the whole take.
- **Approve & Adjust — makes the system match the shelf** — Permission comes from the central matrix cap stockTakeApprove = director only (index.html:966). The saboteur harness deliberately breaks this to check the sentinel bites (test/saboteur-runner.js:108).
- **Reject a stock take** — A rejected take is never re-openable and there is no way for the counter to correct it and resubmit — they have to start a fresh count.
- **Double-tap and double-approve protection** — The clean-take guard was added later than the discrepancy one — it was a real bug found in the Wave G audit (a double-tap appended two stock-take records a millisecond apart).
- **Approval cannot double-apply or resurrect a rejected count** — The idempotency check relies on a field called stockTakeId written onto the adjustment movements. That field is NOT part of the cloud sync contract (sync.js:1287-1320 and 1366-1420 — there is no StockTakeId column), so a device that only received those movements from the cloud would not see the link. Harmless today because stock takes do not sync at all, but it is a live trap for later.
- **Nothing is 'saved' unless it really hit the device** — On failure the app also calls DB.refresh() so the screen does not keep showing a change that was not stored.
- **Email the stock take to Logistics** — THIS EMAIL CANNOT SEND TODAY. The address of the email Logic App is only ever learned from the cloud config channel, and that channel has never worked (sync.js:64 ships CONFIG_URL '%%CONFIG_URL%%'; the fetch bails at sync.js:691-694). With no URL the code posts to 'about:blank' and swallows the error, so the user sees a success toast and nothing is sent, silently. Identical on the live branch (verified against main). The Logic App 'bob-stock-email' is listed in CLAUDE.md as existing in Azure, but I could not verify it is still deployed.
- **The 24-hour Stock Take PIN — set and clear** — MAJOR PRACTICAL GOTCHA: today the PIN is stored ONLY on the device where it was set (a hash in the local 'meta' table — db.js:92, 135, 270, 288). It does not sync anywhere. So a PIN a director sets on their own phone does nothing for the store computer — in practice a director has to walk to each store device and set it there. The cloud version (one PIN, all devices) is built but off. The director should also write the PIN down when the toast appears; it is never shown again.
- **Stock Take locked behind the PIN for shop-floor staff** — The lock only applies to the literal role 'staff'. Store managers, franchisees, TMs, head office and directors go straight in with no PIN. This is a convenience/regression control, not tamper-proof security — a determined person with browser developer tools can bypass it (the project's permanent rule P-13).
- **The PIN grants a temporary permission, not a bypass** — The client half runs today. The server half (the identical rule inside the deployed Function) is on the STAGING Function App only and is never consulted, because no access policy has ever been published.
- **PIN unlock dies on logout, on the idle lock, and when the PIN changes** — The logout and idle-lock clearing works today. The instant kill-switch on Clear PIN needs the cloud policy, which is off. Worth telling Kunal: this answers AA-20, which is DECIDED and built.
- **Server-checked PIN (one PIN for the whole company)** — NOT on the live site at all (it lives only on the working branch). The Function route validatePin IS deployed to the staging Function App (AZURE-CHUNK-AA-STAGING-LEDGER.md:15-17), but the Logic App the client actually calls has not been given the 'pin' operation yet (same file, item 10 in the Remaining list). It would also need internet at the moment of PIN entry — a trade-off Kunal accepted.
- **Password re-confirm before approving a stock take** — Deliberately inert: with no published access policy it returns immediately and never prompts, so today approving asks for nothing. Not on the live branch at all.
- **Who is allowed to count and who is allowed to approve** — The permission list on the live branch is the same for stock takes. The editable-by-a-director version of this matrix, and the matching server enforcement, are built but off. Reminder: these are browser-side checks — good against mistakes and regressions, not against a determined person with developer tools.
- **Discrepancy History report** — Two things to tell Kunal. First, it only shows APPROVED takes with differences (index.html:2638) — pending and rejected counts are invisible here, and clean takes never appear. Second, the '$ impact' is calculated at the RETAIL selling price (index.html:2644 uses the product's price field), not at what the stock cost you — so it reads as lost revenue, not lost cost. There is no CSV export on this page.
- **Stock Reconciliation report** — DEFECT: it picks the latest stock take with no filter on status (index.html:4797) — so a PENDING or REJECTED count is used as the baseline just as readily as an approved one. Since a pending or rejected count was never applied to stock, the whole report would then show phantom discrepancies for that store. Also, 'latest' is decided on the date string only, so two takes on the same day tie and the first one found wins.
- **Negative-stock warning that points at a stock take** — Deliberately does not rewrite the ledger — the underlying history stays intact and the correction goes through a stock take, which leaves an audit trail.
- **Stock takes shared between devices** — NOT on the live site. Today, a stock take exists ONLY on the device it was done on — which is why the app's own sync label says 'Stock last synced' rather than 'Last synced' (index.html:6536-6539): only stock levels and the catalogue travel, not the take records. The two Logic Apps that carry these records were built and proven on STAGING (AZURE-CHUNK4-WAVE-REVIEW.md:9-10) but never went live, and in any case the device would have to be told their addresses via the config channel, which has never worked.
- **Server-side check that the person was allowed to count / approve** — The decision engine is deployed to the staging Function App, but the Logic App that would call it for stock-take steps has NOT been changed (AZURE-CHUNK-AA-STAGING-LEDGER.md:49-53, item 5, listed under Remaining). Until that lands, every permission check on stock takes is browser-side only.
- **Stock-take PIN stripped out of a backup file** — Password and PIN hashes for user accounts ARE still kept in the backup by design (an accepted alpha-stage decision) and the export warns about it.
- **Backup restore checks and store-scopes stock takes** — Note the surrounding comment: stock-take records are deliberately NOT sanitised on load, because stripping characters would orphan them from the movements they created.
- **Out-of-scope stock takes purged from a device** — Only bites once per-account access policies are live, which they are not. Not on the live branch.
- **Half-finished counts cleared when someone logs out** — The flip side is there is no save-and-resume: leave the Stock Take page mid-count, or change store, and your numbers are gone. For a long count on a phone that is a real risk.
- **Stock take record IDs made collision-proof** — The LIVE site does NOT have this — on main the ID is a plain 'st_' plus timestamp (verified: main index.html:2081 and 2145). Two devices creating a take in the same millisecond would collide. Very unlikely, and moot while takes do not sync, but it is a real difference between what is live and what is on the branch.

</details>

| Feature | What it does | Who | Status | Where | Proven by |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Transfers Hub (the transfer list screen)** | One screen listing every transfer this person is allowed to see, newest first, with a coloured status tag (Draft, In Transit, Received, Completed, Cancelled). Tabs across the top filter it: All, In Transit, Needs Receiving, Flagged, Completed, Drafts. 15 per page with Prev/Next. Tapping a row opens the right screen for whatever that transfer needs next — receive it, resolve it, or just view it. | staff (to receive) / store manager / franchisee / territory manager / head office / director | LIVE | `phase2.js:1165-1255 (render), phase2.js:1827-1861 (row tap routing), phase2.js:808-840 (sidebar nav item)` | test/smoke-test.js S-137 (staff sees no Completed tab or completed rows), S-138 (staff blocked from opening a completed transfer), S-130 (franchisee can view history, staff cannot) |
| **New Transfer screen (pick stores, pick products, send)** | Choose a From store and a To store, choose Standard Transfer or Return/Dead Stock, then work down a product list. For each product you see how many the sending store has, how many the receiving store has, that store's optimum level, and the shortfall — then use plus/minus buttons to set how many to send. A search box filters the product list. | **Transit Void — stock leaves the sending store the moment you press Submit** | This is the single most surprising rule in the whole transfer feature. When you submit a transfer, the units are subtracted from the sending store IMMEDIATELY — not when the receiving store confirms them. Between submit and receipt the stock belongs to nobody: it has left one shelf and not yet landed on the other. That is deliberate (the sending store must not be able to sell stock that is physically in a box), but it means the total across all stores dips while goods are on the road, and a transfer that is never received leaves the stock nowhere until someone cancels it or receives it. | everyone — it changes what the stock numbers mean for staff, store managers, franchisees and directors | LIVE | `phase2.js:342-348 (transfer_out written with the transfer, immediate create path), phase2.js:428-435 (same on draft submit), phase2.js:746-751 (cancel writes the units back)` | test/smoke-test.js S-118 (a store can never be driven negative by an over-send), S-121 (cancel refused once someone has received) |
| **Standard Transfer vs Return / Dead Stock** | A toggle on the New Transfer screen. Return/Dead Stock asks for a reason (Damaged, Expired, Excess, Wrong Product, Other) plus free-text notes, and tags the transfer as a Return so the hub shows 'Return' instead of 'Standard'. Mechanically it moves stock exactly the same way — the difference is the label and the reason on the record. | store manager sending goods back / franchisee / head office / director | LIVE | `phase2.js:1320-1344 (toggle + reason), phase2.js:323-325 (type + returnReason stored), phase2.js:1237 (hub label)` | test/smoke-test.js S-120 |

<details><summary>Notes and gotchas (35)</summary>

- **Transfers Hub (the transfer list screen)** — 'Needs Receiving' means 'in transit AND going to one of MY stores'. A Director/Head Office account normally has no stores attached, so that tab shows nothing for them — by design, but it surprises people. No badge colour exists for the newer statuses (conflict / stock_pending / stock_mismatch) so those rows render with an unstyled grey tag.
- **New Transfer screen (pick stores, pick products, send)** — The From store defaults to Head Office for a Director/HO login, otherwise the user's own first store. A non-HO user only sees stores they own in both dropdowns, matching the rule the save enforces. There is no 'Save as draft' button anywhere on this screen — see the Drafts entry.
- **Transit Void — stock leaves the sending store the moment you press Submit** — Cancelling an in-transit transfer is the only way to put the stock back without receiving it, and only a Director can do that.
- **Standard Transfer vs Return / Dead Stock** — The reason is mandatory for a Return — Submit is refused without it.
- **Cannot send more than the sending store has** — This is a check on the device only. Two devices going offline and each sending the same units can still over-send; the real fix is the server-side ledger rule, which is built on the working branch but not live.
- **Quantity rules on every transfer line** — Receiving is different: 0 IS allowed there (nothing turned up), but more than was sent is refused.
- **Draft transfers (build it now, send it later)** — SURPRISE: no button anywhere in the app creates a draft. The New Transfer screen always sends immediately. The only caller that asks for a draft is the auto-draft-from-stock-take function, which nothing calls (see next entry). So the Drafts tab is permanently empty and the Draft screen is unreachable in normal use. Second issue, only visible if a draft ever is created: the Draft screen reads i.qty / i.shortfall to fill its quantity boxes, but saved lines store the number under sentQty (phase2.js:1427,1452 vs phase2.js:326-330) — so every box would open at 0 and 'Shortfall: 0', and pressing Submit without retyping every number would be refused by the whole-number rule.
- **Auto-build a restock transfer from a stock take** — SURPRISE: dead code. A repo-wide search finds no caller anywhere in the app — only the function definition. It was already noted as dead in BLIND-AUDIT-TRACKING.md:401. It would also inherit the draft-screen quantity problem above.
- **Receive Transfer screen (count it in)** — You can only receive a transfer addressed to one of your own stores — checked in the save, not just hidden on screen.
- **Receiving more than was sent is refused** — The plus/minus control on screen still lets you type a higher number — the refusal comes at Submit Receipt with a clear message.
- **A short receipt still credits what actually arrived, straight away** — The record remembers how much was credited at receipt (creditedAtReceive) so the Director's decision only ever moves the difference — no double counting.
- **Same transfer cannot be received twice** — Two layers. The device-side refusal is LIVE today. The authoritative server-side layer (a unique key per transfer+store+product, so the cloud rejects the duplicate even if the two devices never saw each other) is built and proven on the STAGING cloud only (AZURE-CHUNK4-WAVE-REVIEW.md: IdempotencyKey Enforce-Unique on StockTransactions_Validate) and is on the working branch, not the live site.
- **Flagging a discrepancy** — There is no free-text flag note captured at receive time — the note is entered by the Director on the resolve screen.
- **Director resolution — Accept As-Is / Adjust / Reject** — An 'Adjust' downward writes a stock adjustment-out, deliberately NOT a sale — so it never pollutes sales reporting. Adjust is capped at the quantity originally sent.
- **All flagged lines resolved in one all-or-nothing save** — Replaced an older loop that saved each line as it went.
- **Cancel a transfer (Director only)** — The Cancel button only renders for a Director, and the rule is re-checked in the save.
- **All-or-nothing saves with snapshot and restore** — The 'fatal save' overlay forces a reload rather than letting the app continue on a lie (S-72).
- **Email notification when a transfer is sent** — SURPRISE: no transfer email has ever been sendable. The email address for the Logic App is only ever delivered through the cloud config channel, and that channel has never worked (sync.js ships CONFIG_URL as the placeholder '%%CONFIG_URL%%'). With no URL the code logs '[Transfer] email URL not configured' and silently returns (phase2.js:234). The Azure email Logic App itself exists. Also note the send is fire-and-forget — a failure at the email end is never surfaced to the user.
- **Email notification when a transfer completes** — Same blocker as the sent-email: the address never reaches a device. Historical detail worth knowing: this email used to be sent BEFORE the save succeeded, which produced 'ghost' emails for transfers that were never saved; it now fires only after a confirmed write.
- **Who is allowed to do what with transfers** — Because these now route through the central table, a Director will be able to edit them per account once the Account Access feature is switched on. That editing is built but off.
- **You only see and touch your own stores' transfers** — On-device only — it stops accidents and regressions, not a determined person with browser developer tools. The real server-side isolation (Chunk 10) is built and converged on the working branch, not live. Note Transfer.list() itself is only exercised by the test harness; the hub does its own filtering.
- **Read-only transfer view** — This pop-up also carries the two warning banners about stock that has not landed yet (see the reconciliation entry) — but those statuses can only occur once cross-device transfer sync is on, which it is not.
- **Transfers appearing on other devices (cross-device transfer sync)** — Cloud side is BUILT AND PROVEN ON STAGING: a RecordSteps_Staging list plus recordsteps-push/pull Logic Apps, round-trip proven (AZURE-CHUNK-ORG / AZURE-CHUNK4-WAVE-REVIEW.md). Switched OFF for two independent reasons: the code is on the working branch, not the live site; and even on the branch the two step URLs arrive only through the cloud config channel that has never worked, so sync.js:2245 and 2340 quietly do nothing. Drafts deliberately never sync — a draft is private working notes until it is sent.
- **Two devices received the same transfer with different counts (conflict) + the Director's Resolve Receive Conflict screen** — Depends entirely on cross-device step sync above, so it cannot occur today. Non-Directors opening a conflicted transfer see 'Only a Director can resolve a receive conflict.' There is no badge colour defined for the conflict status, so the hub row tag renders unstyled.
- **Warning when a receipt is recorded but the stock has not landed** — Only reachable once cross-device step sync is on. Found by an external auditor: the first build worked this out internally but still displayed 'Completed' on screen.
- **The franchise supply gate — a Head Office to Cockburn transfer can be BLOCKED at submit** — CUTOVER WARNING, already flagged by the repo's own safety check (test/check-publish-safety.js:171): because the cloud config channel has never worked and nothing tells a device its pricing state, the freshness signal never turns true — so the day this branch goes live, EVERY Head Office to franchise transfer would be blocked at submit until the config channel is fixed. Not live today (the gate does not exist on the live site at all).
- **Freezing the franchise price on the transfer at the moment it is sent** — Ground truth for 2026-08-01: dated/era-aware franchise pricing is BUILT AND PROVEN, and switched off only because nothing yet writes or serves a pricing configuration to devices. A retail product with no sell price fails the send closed on purpose, rather than freezing $0 forever. The server columns and signing are applied to the STAGING cloud (AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-STAGING-LEDGER.md).
- **PIN unlock to receive a transfer on a shared store computer** — Completely dormant until a Director publishes an access policy, and that policy is delivered through the config channel that has never worked. Today every logged-in user can receive.
- **Password re-confirmation before resolving a discrepancy** — Does nothing at all until an access policy is published — no prompt appears today.
- **Optimum Stock Levels screen** — Validation: whole non-negative numbers, and the optimum cannot be below the minimum. It preserves the reorder lead-time set on the Settings screen — an earlier version silently wiped it on every Save All.
- **Completed transfers are automatically cleared after 30 days** — SURPRISE, two parts. (1) Kunal should know transfer history self-deletes at 30 days — if he wants a longer paper trail, that number is a one-line change. (2) The automatic version at load looks incomplete: it filters the in-memory list then calls DB.commit(), but DB.commit() rewrites only the reference tables and deliberately excludes the transfers table (db.js:518-531 and db.js:111-131) — so the pruned transfers can reappear after a page reload. The manual pruneCompleted() explicitly deletes them from the device database (phase2.js:787) precisely because of that; the automatic one never got the same fix. Nothing tests either path.
- **Protection against double-tapping the big buttons** — Draft submit is protected differently — by two status checks rather than a flag — and that is documented in the code as deliberate, not an oversight.
- **Half-finished transfer work is wiped at logout** — An earlier version reset only some of the fields — the store selections, transfer type, return reason and threshold edits were missed and were added later.
- **Incoming transfers survive a store-access change** — Working branch only, not live. There is a KNOWN open gap here that is parked, not fixed: the clear-out can run before pending offline transfer writes have been flushed to the cloud, which would lose them (recorded as the offline push-before-purge companion fix in the org-structure work).
- **The plus/minus quantity control used on every transfer screen** — On the Receive, Draft and Adjust screens the maximum is 9999 rather than the sent quantity — the real limits are enforced when you press the submit button, with a message.

</details>

<details><summary>Notes and gotchas (37)</summary>

- **Dashboard (home screen)** — 'Total Stock Value' here is at RETAIL price (Stock.totalValue, index.html:1568-1576) - the SAME words on the Reports page mean cost. Tiles will read 0 / empty because no real movements exist yet.
- **Reports page - date, store, category and product-type filters** — The page itself has NO permission check at the top - the only thing stopping a staff member is that no menu link is shown to them. Client-side only (framework P-13).
- **Reports > Overview tab (stat tiles + two charts)** — SURPRISE: the tile labelled 'Total Stock Value' here is valued at COST (Stock.currentCost, index.html:3284), while the identically-labelled tile on the Dashboard and Stock Overview is valued at RETAIL. Two screens, same words, different number. Also this tile silently includes the HO warehouse and the franchise office when no store filter is set.
- **Reports > Transaction Log tab** — Silently capped at the newest 500 rows with no 'showing 500 of N' message. Deleted movements (tombstones) still appear here as a blank '—' row; they do not affect any total (S-80 in test/smoke-test.js proves they are inert in the maths).
- **Reports > Stock Snapshot tab** — Ignores the From/To dates entirely - it is always 'now', which is easy to misread when a date range is set above it.
- **Reports > Category Analysis tab** — The pie only appears once a filter is chosen - with no filter you get the table only, which looks like a missing chart.
- **All Transactions CSV export** — GOTCHA: this CSV only honours the STORE and DATE filters. The category and product-type filters on screen are ignored, so the spreadsheet can contain rows the screen was hiding. It also includes deleted tombstone rows.
- **Current Stock CSV export** — The file itself is live today. The seeSellingPrice gate that hides the price column (S-248) is only on the working branch and only bites once the access policy is switched on - which needs the cloud config channel that has never worked.
- **Movements CSV export (full ledger)** — Unlike the Reports CSV, this one correctly drops deleted tombstones and exports ALL dates with no filter at all.
- **Products CSV export** — Exports the per-product franchise discount, so this is the closest thing to a printable record of the discount rules.
- **Print report** — Only the Reports page has a print button; no other report offers one.
- **Store Comparison (period A vs period B)** — CAUTION: the 'Value' charts and the A/B Value columns multiply units by the CURRENT catalogue price, so raising a price today changes what last year appears to have been worth. The Director-only Gross Sales column does it properly (frozen price). Only a Director sees the Gross Sales / Total Cost / on-hand-at-cost columns; everyone else sees units and retail value only.
- **Store Comparison IN / OUT / BOTH toggle and QTY / VALUE trend switch** — Defaults to OUT and to QTY.
- **Sell-Through Rate report** — Deliberately excludes consumables (professional products used in treatments) because they are used, never sold. Table capped at 200 rows.
- **Wastage & Damage report + CSV** — Items with no cost recorded are shown as '— no cost' and counted in a warning, never quietly treated as $0. Director-only: index.html:4428 redirects everyone else.
- **Dead Stock / Aging report** — Values stock at RETAIL price ('Est. Retail Value Tied Up') while the Wastage report values at cost - the two reports are not comparable. Table capped at 200 rows. Any OUT counts as movement, including a transfer out, so shipping stock away makes a dead item look alive.
- **Stock Reconciliation report** — Only uses the single most recent take per store. Table capped at 200 rows.
- **Discrepancy History report** — Only counts APPROVED stock takes - a pending or rejected one never appears. Values at current retail price.
- **Stock Overview (all locations on hand)** — Valued at RETAIL price. A franchisee only ever sees their own stores and never the HO warehouse column.
- **Current Stock (single store view)** — If the account covers more than one store this page politely refuses and points at Comparison / Alerts / Reports (index.html:2314-2322).
- **All Movements (movement history, all stores)** — Opens defaulted to the last 90 days and shows the newest 500 rows only, with no warning that it is truncated. The type filter matches the raw type only, so 'Stock OUT' misses wastage and transfer-out rows.
- **Transaction History (single store)** — Not shown to staff. Refuses to load for a multi-store account.
- **Low Stock Alerts + Copy Summary** — Copy Summary always copies ALL stores, ignoring the store filter you have selected on screen (index.html:2737).
- **Negative stock banner (Director)** — Deliberately Director-only - staff and franchisees see those products as simply out of stock.
- **Reorder List + CSV** — Suggested quantity is a simple formula (top back up to twice the minimum, never less than the minimum) - it is not based on sales speed. Only products that have a threshold set for that store appear at all.
- **Margin Report** — Hidden inside Settings rather than under Analytics, so it is easy to miss. Shows 'No data' for any product with no recorded delivery cost, and tells you to record deliveries in Cost Management.
- **FRANCHISE INVOICE REPORT (the money report)** — ⚠ THE LIVE VERSION IS THE OLD ONE. On the live site every line is priced from TODAY's catalogue price and TODAY's discount, so changing Cockburn's discount or a product price silently rewrites every past invoice (verified: main branch index.html:4120-4145 has no dated pricing at all). Also: it bills the Cockburn Franchise OFFICE only - anything Head Office ships straight to the Cockburn shop never appears on any invoice.
- **Franchise Invoice CSV export** — Refuses with 'Open the report first' if you have not loaded the report - it reads the dates the screen stashed (index.html:4976).
- **Era-aware (dated) franchise discount on the invoice** — BUILT AND PROVEN BUT OFF. It is not on the live site, and even on the working branch nothing writes or serves a pricing_config, so the lens never activates and the app runs the old single-rate maths. Two further gaps: there is no server route to CHANGE a rate (HANDOVER.md:655-656), and Kunal's decided model - a universal store rate, overridden by a PRODUCT TYPE rate, overridden by a per-product rate - has no by-type tier anywhere in the code.
- **Frozen price and discount stamps on supplied stock** — Working branch only - not on the live site. A stamped 0% is deliberately shown as a loud 'NOT SET' rather than a silent full-price line.
- **Archive-aware reporting (banner + Load archived data)** — Working branch only - the live site has no archive awareness at all. Deliberately fails safe: it warns rather than quietly under-counting.
- **Buy-back settlement export (server engine)** — The route is deployed on the staging Function App (HANDOVER.md:17, 14 routes) but nothing calls it - there is no screen, no button and no Logic App gate. Two known money defects are parked against it: an edit made directly in SharePoint can slip past the seal and settle at $3.75 instead of $375 (HANDOVER.md:431-437), and an id-format clash aborts the whole store export (HANDOVER.md:456-459). Kunal parked this whole item on 2026-07-29.
- **Money definitions shared by every report (Gross Sales, Total Cost, on-hand at cost)** — Deliberately NO profit or P&L figure anywhere - an earlier version wrongly counted every outflow as profit and that was removed. The blue 'Updated Jun 2026' notes on the Comparison, Wastage and Sell-Through pages explain this to users.
- **Audit Log (deleted movements)** — No CSV export and no date filter - store filter only.
- **Login Audit** — Per-device only - it does NOT show logins made on other devices, and the screen says so.
- **Who can see which report (the permission gates)** — BUILT, PROVEN, DEPLOYED - AND OFF. Not on the live site, and even on the working branch it stays dormant until a Director publishes a policy, which needs the cloud config channel that has never worked. Today the older hard-coded role checks govern. And this is convenience, not security: anyone with browser developer tools can bypass a browser-side check (project rule P-13).
- **Spreadsheet safety on every CSV** — One shared helper, so a new export gets the protection for free.

</details>

<details><summary>Notes and gotchas (29)</summary>

- **The product list (catalogue)** — All 192 products are practice data. Only 51 of them have a sell price filled in — the other 141 show an amber "⚠ Missing" warning on the Products screen.
- **Product Management screen (list, filter, search)** — The header says "N active products" — deactivated (removed) products are hidden from this table but still exist in the data.
- **Add a product** — Codes are forced to CAPITALS and may only use letters, numbers, _ and -. If a head-office user (not a director) sets a franchise discount, it is silently dropped — a director must set it later (index.html:3480).
- **Edit a product** — The edit form deliberately does NOT show supplier, lead days, minimum levels or cost — those live on other screens.
- **Remove a product** — TWO gotchas. (1) It is more destructive than it sounds — cost history and stock-take lines for that product are permanently deleted from the device. (2) It is never sent to the cloud: unlike removing a category or a product type, this action does not flag the change for publishing, so other devices would keep showing the product forever.
- **Retail (sell) price** — Prices are capped at $1,000,000 on the device, and refused if they have more than 2 decimal places. The cloud checker allows up to $10,000,000, so the device is the stricter of the two.
- **Retail vs Consumable (stock type)** — None of the 192 seeded products has this set, so today every product behaves as Retail by default. An older "internal use" tick box was retired — the dropdown is now the only thing that matters.
- **Product Types (Retail / Treatment / Cleaning / Stationery)** — A product's type is NOT set on the product — it comes from its category. To move a product between types you change its category. Also: "Remove" only deactivates, and the row keeps appearing in this list looking identical to a live one.
- **Categories** — Same gotcha as product types: a "removed" category still shows in the Categories list with Edit and Remove buttons. It only disappears from the dropdowns where you would pick it.
- **Minimum and optimum stock levels per store (Reorder Thresholds screen)** — THE BIG ONE: these numbers never leave the device. "thresholds" appears nowhere in the sync engine — set minimums on the office PC and the Karrinyup phone will not see them, ever. Also, the Lead Days column on this screen does NOT exist on the live site (it is only on the unreleased working branch) — today lead days can only be set on the Suppliers screen, per product, not per store.
- **Optimum Stock Levels bulk editor (a second, separate screen)** — Confusing by design: two screens edit the same numbers with slightly different rules, and this one can set a "Global" level that applies to every store. Also device-local — nothing here syncs.
- **How the app decides which minimum applies** — A product with no minimum set anywhere is silently excluded from low-stock alerts and the reorder list — it can run to zero with no warning at all. On the Stock Overview screen it is at least labelled "No threshold".
- **Low-stock colour badges** — A grey badge with a bare number means "no minimum has been set for this product here".
- **Low Stock Alerts screen** — The Head Office warehouse is excluded from these alerts (only shops and the franchise office are included). Because the minimums never sync, two devices can legitimately show completely different alert lists.
- **Copy low-stock summary to clipboard** — It copies alerts for ALL stores regardless of the store filter currently selected on screen.
- **Store stock filters (type chips, Low Stock Only, level bar)** — The progress bar is scaled against three times the minimum, so a product exactly on its minimum shows about a third full.
- **Stock Overview status column (head office / director view)** — Negative stock is shown as zero dollars rather than negative dollars, on purpose.
- **Reorder List (what to order, grouped by supplier)** — Only products with a minimum set appear here. Products with no supplier name land under a "No Supplier" heading. Lead days prefer the product-level value, falling back to the store-level one.
- **Supplier Management (supplier, contact, lead days per product)** — Saved to the device only — this screen does not flag the change for publishing, so supplier details never reach other devices on their own. They only travel if someone later edits that product's name, price or category, which flags the whole row.
- **Products list CSV export** — The export itself is live; the price-hiding rule attached to it is only on the unreleased working branch.
- **Franchise discount per product** — Two gaps. (1) Kunal's decided model is store rate → PRODUCT TYPE rate → product rate. The product-type tier does not exist at all; there is only store rate then per-product. (2) Setting a discount is saved to the device but is never flagged for publishing, so on its own it never reaches another device.
- **Dated / era-aware franchise pricing (the "pricing lens")** — BUILT AND PROVEN BUT SWITCHED OFF. Nothing writes or serves a pricing_config, and the settings channel that would deliver one has never worked, so the app quietly falls back to the single-number discount. The lookup order it implements is: this store's rate for this product → the company-wide rate for this product → this store's default rate. There is no product-type tier.
- **Who is allowed to see prices and costs on the product screen** — This whole permissions layer is on the unreleased working branch and is inert until a director publishes an access policy — which needs the settings channel that has never worked. On the live site today, everyone who can reach the Products screen sees the sell price.
- **Cost Price column on the product screen** — It is display-only here — cost is actually set on the Deliveries / Cost screens. Cost is deliberately never included in the shared product catalogue: the cloud strips it out of every published product row (catalogueMerge.js:69-79, verified by running it today).
- **Publish Catalogue (sending product changes to every device)** — Entirely absent from the live site — the whole publishing mechanism only exists on the unreleased working branch. Three catalogue actions forget to add themselves to the waiting list at all: removing a product, changing a product's franchise discount, and saving supplier details.
- **Cloud checks on published catalogue changes** — The Function is deployed to the staging cloud (one of the 10 routes on bob-stock-money-fn). The per-role permission check on the wrapping Logic App is still on the to-do list (AZURE-CHUNK-AA-STAGING-LEDGER.md item 9). It is switched off in practice because no device is running the client half.
- **Receiving catalogue updates from the cloud** — HAS NEVER RUN ANYWHERE. The settings address it depends on was never filled in at build time (sync.js:64 still reads '%%CONFIG_URL%%'), so the fetch gives up immediately at sync.js:691. No shared product list has ever reached any device.
- **Guards that stop the catalogue breaking itself** — The in-use check is deliberately run twice — once when you click, once again just before saving — so leaving the confirm box open while someone else adds a product cannot slip through.
- **Unused fields carried on every product record** — Harmless, but misleading to anyone reading the data or a backup file — a per-product weight looks like it exists and does not.

</details>

<details><summary>Notes and gotchas (33)</summary>

- **Cost price on a product (the current cost)** — Live on the site today, but EVERY one of the 192 seeded products has costPrice: null — so there is no cost on file for anything, and every margin/cost figure currently reads $0 or 'No cost'. Server side: cost can be delivered by the gated corporate-costs route on staging, but that route's account-level permission check has not been applied yet.
- **Cost history — the dated record of what things used to cost** — SURPRISE: cost history never leaves the device. Nothing pushes or pulls it, and when a delivery syncs to another device the fold rebuilds the delivery record but NOT the cost-history rows (records.js:604-608). Two devices would end up with different dated cost histories. Only the single current cost can travel, via the corporate-costs blob.
- **Add a cost entry by hand (+ Cost)** — Also flags the product as needing a catalogue publish (Sync._markCatalogueDirty('cost', ...)) — but that publish path does not exist on the live site.
- **View and remove cost history for a product** — Removal is permanent and there is no undo — it just asks for confirmation. Deleting a product also silently wipes that product's whole cost history (index.html:3520).
- **Cost Management screen** — Entry is gated on the viewReports permission (Director only). Today the counters would read '0/192 products with cost data' and '192 products missing cost'.
- **Record a Delivery — the landed-cost calculator** — Always lands the stock into 'head_office' — you cannot record a delivery straight into a shop. Saving is one atomic write: stock, delivery, cost history and product cost all land together or none do.
- **Shipment-wide charges: Shipping, Freight, and Tax & Duty** — There is NO separate 'Customs' box — customs is meant to be entered inside 'Tax & Duty'. Older records could carry a customs value and the screens still display it if present (index.html:5617, :5668-5669), but nothing can create one any more.
- **Deliveries in a foreign currency** — The rate is typed in by hand — there is no live exchange-rate lookup. If you later edit packaging on a foreign delivery, the edit must be entered in AUD; the screen warns you (index.html:5667).
- **Higher-wins cost rule** — SURPRISE worth Kunal's confirmation: this means a genuinely cheaper supplier will never bring the recorded cost down, so margins stay pessimistic forever until someone adds a manual cost entry.
- **Delivery History and delivery detail** — Deliveries themselves DO sync between devices on the working branch, via the record-steps channel (records.js:604-608) whose server side is deployed on staging. That channel is not on the live site.
- **Edit Packaging & Labelling on a past delivery** — Only packaging and labelling can be changed after the fact. Unit cost, freight, tax, shipping, quantity and weight are fixed once the delivery is saved.
- **Money guardrails on every cost field** — The exchange rate deliberately uses a different rule from money (more decimal places, own upper bound of 100,000) because rounding a rate like 0.6523 to two decimals would corrupt the conversion.
- **Server-side delivery money check** — Deployed on the staging Function App and genuinely wired into the staging ingest Logic App. It is switched off in practice because the client that emits delivery record-steps is not on the live site.
- **Margins tab (Settings > Margins)** — With no cost data on file today, the whole table reads 'No data' and the prompt at index.html:4370 tells you to record deliveries first.
- **Margin badge on the Cost Management table** — A past bug read a field called sellPrice that never existed, so margins were always blank; the sentinel exists to stop that coming back.
- **Franchise discount — per product** — There is no 0% option — a blank means 'inherit the store rate', so a genuine 0% (full price) discount cannot be expressed at product level. On the working branch this edit becomes a server call once dated pricing is switched on; today it just saves locally.
- **Franchise discount — the store/office-wide rate** — SURPRISE: there is NO screen anywhere to set or change this. The 25% on Cockburn is hard-coded seed data. Any new franchise store is created with no rate, and the invoice then bills it at 0% (loudly flagged as 'NOT SET', never silently). Also: Kunal's decided model has a middle tier — a rate per PRODUCT TYPE (retail 50% vs professional ~25%) — and that tier does not exist in the code at all.
- **Franchise transfer pricing preview on Log Movement** — If no sell price or no discount is configured it just says 'No sell price or discount configured — set in Products & Stores'.
- **Franchise Invoice Report** — An office with no discount set is billed at 0% but is LOUDLY flagged as 'NOT SET' — never silently full price. Returns and credits are explicitly out of scope and handled separately.
- **Frozen invoice pricing (price and discount stamped at the moment of supply)** — Not on the live site (main has no sellAtSupply anywhere). All four stamp values must be present or the line is treated as broken and fails safe — a half-stamp is never trusted.
- **Dated / era-aware franchise pricing (the pricing lens)** — BUILT AND OFF, not missing. Nothing has ever written or served a pricing_config: the config channel itself is dead (sync.js:64 still ships the placeholder '%%CONFIG_URL%%' and sync.js:691 bails), and no pricing Logic App exists on staging (zero mentions of pricing in AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-STAGING-LEDGER.md). Until then the app quietly uses the old single-percentage behaviour, byte-for-byte.
- **Server pricing engine (as-of rates, append-only history, era alignment)** — The topologyPlan/topologyResolve routes went live on the staging Function App as a side effect of the 2026-07-23 deploy (14 routes total), but no Logic App calls them and no pricing data exists for them to read.
- **Pricing commit gate — blocks franchise supply from a stale device** — Completely inert until dated pricing is switched on — before activation the gate passes straight through, so nothing is blocked today.
- **Publish a pricing change to the server** — The endpoint does not exist anywhere — not on staging, not in production. Every call would return 'no-endpoint', which is why this path only runs after dated pricing is activated.
- **Who can see cost prices, and who can see selling prices** — Not on the live site at all — main has no seeCost. On the working branch these are inert until a Director publishes the first access policy. Note the honest limitation recorded in the code: selling price is a view hint only (it is public catalogue data); cost is the one the server really enforces.
- **Cost is stripped out of the shared product catalogue** — The server half is deployed on staging. None of it is on the live site (main's sync.js has no catalogue publish at all).
- **Corporate costs served separately and permission-checked** — The Logic App exists and works on staging, but it currently gates on the device key only. The account-level 'see cost prices' check is still on the not-yet-applied list (AZURE-CHUNK-AA-STAGING-LEDGER.md item 6). Also switched off in practice because the app can never learn the endpoint's address — the config channel has never worked.
- **Cost is wiped from a device when cost access is taken away** — Client-side only by design — the server cannot reach into a device. Not on the live site; inert until an access policy is published. Deliberate limitation recorded in the code: your OWN locally-typed cost history is not wiped, only the company payload the server sent you.
- **Cost-based management reports** — Deliberately no profit or P&L figure anywhere — an earlier version wrongly counted every outflow as profit and it was removed. Items with no cost on file are counted as missing and flagged ('⚠ N w/o cost'), never as $0. With no cost data today every one of these reads zero.
- **Sell price frozen at the moment of sale** — The code comment at index.html:2192 is now out of date — it says the snapshot is 'NOT pushed to SharePoint yet', but the UnitPriceAtTime column WAS added to the staging lists on 2026-07-23 (AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-STAGING-LEDGER.md, W4.4 Contract 1). Doc vs code disagreement worth fixing.
- **Stock value — at retail on most screens, at cost on one** — SURPRISE: this will confuse anyone comparing the Dashboard with Reports. Both say 'Total Stock Value'. Neither says which basis it is on.
- **Suppliers and lead times** — The Reorder List shows no money at all — quantities and lead days only, no cost of the suggested order.
- **Buy-back export — the money owed when Head Office takes a franchise store back** — The route went live on the staging Function App with the 2026-07-23 deploy, but no Logic App or screen calls it yet and there is no button anywhere in the app.

</details>
