<details><summary>Notes and gotchas (32)</summary>

- **User Management screen (Settings → Users)** — Head Office CANNOT open this — there is no Users link in the Head Office menu and the page bounces you to Log Movement if you try. When cloud accounts are eventually switched on the screen gains a Status column (OK / failed attempts / 🔒 Locked / Inactive) and refreshes itself from the cloud; today that whole column is hidden.
- **Add a user** — THREE gotchas. (1) If the PIN box is left blank the app silently sets that person's PIN to 1234 (index.html:3701) — no warning, no note on screen. (2) There is no minimum password length and no check on what characters a username may contain; those rules exist only on the not-yet-live cloud path (8 characters, 10 for Directors). (3) The new account exists only on the device that created it — adding a user does not send them to any other device.
- **Edit a user (name, username, password, PIN, role, shops)** — Taking a username someone else already holds is refused, and the last remaining Director cannot be demoted. Gotcha: promoting someone to Director or Head Office hides the shop tick-boxes but does NOT clear ticks already made — the old shop list is quietly saved with the account.
- **Delete a user (becomes Deactivate once cloud accounts are on)** — You cannot delete your own account, and you cannot delete the last Director. The delete only affects the device you are standing at — it does not remove anything from the cloud, and nothing stops that person logging in on another device.
- **Unlock a locked-out account / Reactivate a switched-off account** — Completely invisible today. The buttons only appear when the app can see the cloud account service, and it never can — see the config-channel note. There is no lockout at all on the live app: passwords can be guessed forever.
- **The six roles and what each one can do** — The six role names are fixed in the code — a Director cannot invent a new role from the app. "Staff" really means the shared shop computer, not an individual person. Also worth knowing: these checks live in the browser, so they stop honest mistakes, not a determined person with developer tools — that is exactly what the server phase is for.
- **Assigning which shops a person can see** — Warehouses are deliberately kept out of the tick list, so nobody can be attached to the Head Office warehouse. The Cockburn Franchise Office is the one exception, because it carries the special "franchise office" flag.
- **Passwords** — switchedOn = no refers to the cloud-checked password only; the device-side password check IS live. The live one has no minimum length and no lockout.
- **Screen lock after 10 minutes, opened with a personal 4-digit PIN** — Real gotcha: the lock screen has NO way out except the correct PIN — no Logout, no Cancel. If a person has no PIN it just says "No PIN set. Contact admin." and they are stuck (reloading the page is the only escape). And note that when cloud accounts are switched on, users created that way get NO personal PIN at all (index.html:3715) — so every one of them would hit that dead end on the first idle lock.
- **"Confirm your password" re-prompt before risky actions** — Today it is completely silent — line 4271 detects that cloud accounts are off and waves every action straight through with no prompt. Nobody has ever seen this box.
- **Logging in** — The login screen is live. The cloud half of it — the server confirming who you are and telling the app what role you hold — is built, deployed to the staging cloud and tested, but never runs on a device.
- **Login Audit** — It only shows logins made on the device you are looking at — the on-screen banner says so. It is not a company-wide login log. The menu link existed for a long time with no page behind it; the route was only wired up in Wave G.
- **Account Access screen — the permission grid** — BUILT AND OFF. The screen renders, but it shows the amber banner "⚠ Access policy NOT ACTIVE" and pressing Publish returns "endpoint not configured". The Function routes and the write Logic App are deployed on the staging cloud and proven to refuse an unauthorised device, but the other Logic Apps that must also honour the policy are not applied yet (AZURE-CHUNK-AA-STAGING-LEDGER.md:47-60). Note for training: switching it on deliberately changes one thing — a shop account will need the 24-hour PIN to receive a Head Office delivery.
- **24-hour Stock Take PIN** — Big practical limit today: the PIN is saved and checked on the ONE device that set it. A PIN set on the Booragoon computer does not reach Claremont's computer. The all-shops version (PIN kept in the cloud, checked online) is built and tested but off. Also note the panel says "Current PIN: PIN is active" — it deliberately never shows you the number again, so write it down.
- **Store Management screen** — Head Office reaches it from their own "Stores" menu item; Directors reach it from Settings → Stores. Both can add, edit and deactivate.
- **Add a store** — THIS IS THE WHOLE FORM — three fields. You cannot set a franchise discount, cannot mark it as a franchise office, and cannot say who owns it. New shops are created with no discount at all. Second gotcha: the ID rules on the device are looser than the cloud's (the cloud only accepts letters, numbers, _ and -, catalogueMerge.js:44), so an ID containing a full stop or an accent saves happily on the device and is then silently refused when you publish.
- **Edit a store** — SERIOUS GOTCHA. The save line recomputes "is this a franchise?" purely from the type dropdown (index.html:3552). The Cockburn Franchise Office has type Warehouse but IS a franchise. So opening Edit on the Cockburn Franchise Office and pressing Save — even changing nothing — turns its franchise flag OFF, and it drops out of Franchise Invoicing. Also: the shop's ID can never be changed, and the franchise discount is not on the form at all.
- **Activate / deactivate a store** — There is no way to permanently delete a shop from inside the app — the Store Management screen only offers Deactivate. Ardross and Bunbury were removed on 2026-08-02 by editing the seed directly, which is only safe because the app has never been used and they had no real history. Once the app is live, closing a shop must mean deactivate, never delete. Separately: once cloud delivery of the shop list works, removing a shop from the cloud list will NOT remove it from a device — the merge only adds and updates.
- **Store types (Inline / Kiosk / Franchise / Online / Warehouse)** — Worth saying out loud in training: choosing Kiosk instead of Inline changes nothing about how the app behaves. Choosing Warehouse does — that location vanishes from staff-facing shop pickers.
- **The "is a franchise" flag** — It cannot be ticked on its own — it is set automatically from the type dropdown, which is what causes the Franchise Office bug above.
- **The "franchise office" flag — the entity you actually invoice** — THE BIG GAP IN THIS AREA. Nothing anywhere in the app can create or set a franchise office — not the Add Store form, not the Edit Store form, not the cloud. The single one that exists was typed into the starting data. So a new franchisee cannot be onboarded from the app at all today. The cloud's shop validation does not even know the field exists (catalogueMerge.js:59-64).
- **A store's franchise discount (what the franchisee pays)** — THERE IS NO EDITOR. The 25% on Cockburn is a number typed into the starting data — no screen, no form, no cloud call can change it. The cloud would happily accept a new value and the invoice would use it; nothing can send one. And the model Kunal settled on today (a shop-wide rate, overridden by a PRODUCT TYPE rate, overridden by a per-product rate) has no product-type tier anywhere in the code — that middle level does not exist. Cockburn's real rates (Retail 50%, professional ~25%) cannot be expressed at all.
- **Per-product franchise discount** — Fixed steps only — you cannot type 12.5%. A red ⚠ appears next to any product where neither the product nor the shop has a rate, because that line would bill at zero discount.
- **Dated franchise pricing — a rate history instead of one number** — BUILT AND SWITCHED OFF — do not report this as missing. It is the intended home for the store franchise discount. It is dormant because nothing writes or serves a pricing file: the Logic App that would accept a rate change was never built (AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-STAGING-LEDGER.md:105) and the settings channel that would deliver one has never worked. Until then the app falls back to the single seeded number.
- **The store / franchise change wizard (server half only — no screen)** — There is NO SCREEN for any of this — not a button, not a menu item. The whole thing is server logic with nothing in front of it. The routes are live on the staging cloud (AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-STAGING-LEDGER.md:17, 14 routes) but no Logic App calls them and no device knows they exist. This is what would be needed for the two real cases due around Sep-Oct 2026.
- **Store isolation — a device only holds its own shop's data** — Not on the live app at all (this code only exists on the working branch), and the cloud never sends the shop list it depends on. If it fails to wipe cleanly it deliberately blocks backup exports until it succeeds.
- **Sync Keys — authorising a device for a shop** — Not on the live app. Even on the working branch it does nothing useful yet, because the address the keys would be sent to is never delivered.
- **Publishing store changes out to every device** — IMPORTANT FOR TRAINING: users are NOT part of publishing. Only products, categories, product types and shops travel. A person added on the Karrinyup computer will never appear on the Whitford computer by publishing — accounts only spread once cloud accounts are switched on.
- **Backup file — what happens to users and stores inside it** — REAL GOTCHA. Because password and PIN scrambles are stripped, restoring a backup onto a BRAND NEW device gives you the full user list with no way for anyone to log in (the device keeps its own login check separately, which is why restoring onto the same device is fine). The comment in the code at line 3928 still claims the hashes are "intentionally kept" — that comment is out of date; the code strips them. Worth testing before relying on a backup as the disaster plan.
- **The stores and accounts the app starts with** — This list is WRONG and it is live on both branches. It still includes Ardross and Bunbury, both closed. It does not include the store-ownership facts. Every one of the 16 accounts already has a password and a PIN set. None of this is real data — nothing in the app has ever been used in anger.
- **Cloud account administration (the server side of user management)** — Deployed and tested on the staging cloud, including a guard that refuses to remove or demote the last Director. It is off purely because no device is ever told its address.
- **Why none of the cloud user/store features are on — the settings channel** — This one line is the single reason the Account Access screen says NOT ACTIVE, the cloud login never runs, the password re-prompt never appears, the shop list never syncs, and the dated pricing sits dormant. Fixing it is a build-pipeline step, not app code.

</details>

## <a id="sync-offline"></a>Sync, offline and backup

| Feature | What it does | Who | Status | Where | Proven by |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Offline-first: the app saves to the device before it saves to the cloud** | Everything staff type — a sale, a wastage, a delivery, a stock take — is written into a small database inside the browser on that phone or tablet first. The screen updates instantly and nothing waits for the internet. The cloud is a second step that happens quietly afterwards. That is why the app keeps working in a salon with bad wifi, and why nothing is lost if the wifi drops mid-shift. | staff / store manager / franchisee / director / head office — everyone, all the time | LIVE | `db.js:23 (local database defined), db.js:310 DB.get() reads from memory, db.js:1053-1092 durable write helpers, db.js:1366 initDB loads everything before the first screen paints` | test/smoke-test.js S-01 (a failed local write returns false, never a fake success), S-11 (device storage full → write fails cleanly and rolls back), S-16, S-126 |
| **'You are offline' red bar** | A red strip appears across the top of the screen the moment the device loses internet, saying changes are saved locally and will sync when reconnected. It disappears by itself when the connection returns. | staff / store manager / franchisee / director | LIVE | `index.html:6513-6516 (the bar), index.html:6532-6535 updateOnlineStatus(), index.html:6566 offline listener` | NONE |
| **Automatic background sync (save → 0.8s pause → send; plus a check every 30 seconds)** | After anyone records something, the app waits about a second (so a burst of quick entries goes as one batch) and then sends it up. Separately, one tab on the device checks the cloud for other stores' changes every 30 seconds. Nobody has to press anything. | staff / store manager / franchisee / director / head office | BUILT + PROVEN, OFF | `sync.js:2458 scheduleSync(), sync.js:61 DEBOUNCE_MS 800, sync.js:60 POLL_INTERVAL 30000, sync.js:2544 poll(), sync.js:1238 _startPolling()` | test/smoke-test.js S-79 (the 30s cycle also pushes anything waiting, not just pulls), S-123 (transfer/delivery writes each trigger a sync) |
| **'Sync now' button and the 'Stock last synced' label** | A ⟳ Sync now button in the sidebar (and a ⟳ Sync button in the mobile top bar) forces an immediate send-and-fetch. Underneath it a line reads 'Stock last synced: 4m ago' so a manager can see at a glance whether the numbers on screen are current. | staff / store manager / franchisee / director | BUILT + PROVEN, OFF | `index.html:6094 (sidebar button), index.html:340 (mobile button), index.html:6551-6557 triggerManualSync(), index.html:6539-6550 the label, sync.js:2483 syncNow()` | test/smoke-test.js S-124 (the button runs send-then-fetch on the syncing tab and is delegated, never duplicated, from other tabs), S-125 (the label's clock is a display-only stamp and can never corrupt the real sync position), S-128 |
| **Auto-sync the moment the internet comes back** | When the device reconnects, the app waits 2.5 seconds (so a flapping connection cannot trigger a storm) and then automatically sends everything that piled up while offline. | staff / store manager / franchisee | BUILT + PROVEN, OFF | `index.html:6558-6565 (online listener, debounced)` | test/smoke-test.js S-128 (a reconnect sync that collides with a sync already running is queued, not dropped) |
| **Sending work up (push) with an honest receipt from the server** | When the app sends movements up, the server replies row by row: accepted, already had it, permanently rejected, or temporarily failed. The app only ticks a row off as 'sent' when the server actually confirms it. If the reply is vague, contradictory, or leaves any row unaccounted for, the app refuses to tick anything off and tries again — it will never show 'Synced ✓' over work that did not land. | staff / store manager / franchisee / director (invisible, but it is what makes the numbers trustworthy) | BUILT + PROVEN, OFF | `sync.js:1498 push(), sync.js:1620-1706 (the four-bucket receipt), sync.js:1707-1780 (the older simpler receipt, kept as a fallback)` | test/smoke-test.js S-163, S-164, S-165, S-166, S-167, S-168 (a row in no bucket forces a retry), S-169, S-170 (a contradictory reply is trusted for nothing), S-02, S-42, S-67 |
| **Junk never leaves the device (egress check)** | Before anything is sent to the cloud the app re-checks each row against the same rules it uses when data arrives: sensible quantity, a real product, a real store. Anything that fails is held back and flagged, so one corrupted row on one tablet can never poison every other store's numbers. | director / head office (protects everyone) | BUILT + PROVEN, OFF | `sync.js:1536-1563 (_egressOk and the excluded-row flagging)` | test/smoke-test.js S-52 (only the valid row is sent), S-65 (a properly formed offline delete is allowed through, a malformed one is not) |
| **Fetching other stores' work down (pull), paged safely** | The app asks the cloud 'what is new since row number X?' and walks forward a thousand rows at a time. It freezes a ceiling at the start of each round so rows arriving mid-fetch cannot make the walk go on forever, and it deliberately re-reads the last hundred rows each time in case one was still being written last round. It only moves its bookmark forward once every page has been safely saved to the device — so a failure mid-fetch loses nothing, it just re-fetches. | staff / store manager / franchisee / director / head office | BUILT + PROVEN, OFF | `sync.js:1861 pull(), sync.js:1850 PULL_PAGE_SIZE 1000, sync.js:1851 PULL_ID_LOOKBACK 100, sync.js:2141-2151 (bookmark only advances after a durable save)` | test/smoke-test.js S-159, S-160, S-161, S-162 (a nonsense server page aborts the round rather than half-applying), S-04, S-20 (a quiet system still moves its bookmark so it does not re-ask the same question forever) |
| **Bad data arriving from the cloud is quarantined, not swallowed** | If a row comes down with a negative quantity, a nonsense product, or an ID containing characters that could break the app, it is put aside and logged rather than merged. The app never quietly rounds or 'fixes' a bad number, because a silently fixed number is a wrong number nobody notices. | director / head office | BUILT + PROVEN, OFF | `sync.js:2027-2074 (the ingest loop), sync.js:1332 _fromSharePoint (strict rules, returns nothing for a bad row)` | test/smoke-test.js S-47, S-51, S-83 |
| **Deletes travel between devices (tombstones), including deletes made offline** | When someone deletes a movement, the app does not just remove it — it writes a small 'this was deleted' marker that syncs like any other row. That is how the delete reaches the other stores' devices. It works offline too: the marker sits in the queue until there is internet. Old markers that have already synced are tidied away after 30 days. | store manager / franchisee / director | BUILT + PROVEN, OFF | `db.js:888 _makeTombstone, db.js:1135 removeTransactionDurable, sync.js:2101-2119 (applying incoming deletes), db.js:702 pruneSyncedTombstones` | test/smoke-test.js S-65, S-74 (a failed delete rolls back both halves — no orphan marker), S-75 (a delete with no cloud configured still leaves a durable queued marker), S-76 (a create and delete arriving in the same batch does not resurrect the row), S-77, S-78, S-80 |
| **The offline queue survives closing the app** | There is no separate 'outbox' screen — unsent work is simply every row on the device not yet ticked as sent. A flag is kept on the device saying 'something is still waiting', so if a manager closes the app on Friday with unsent work and reopens it Monday, the app pushes it at launch before doing anything else. | staff / store manager / franchisee | BUILT + PROVEN, OFF | `sync.js:969-970 (the waiting flag), sync.js:2622-2630 (pushes it at launch), sync.js:2552-2559 (the 30s cycle also drains it)` | test/smoke-test.js S-79, S-75 |
| **Retry with a growing wait, so a flaky connection heals itself** | If a send fails, the app retries after 2 seconds, then 4, then 6, showing 'Sync failed, retrying (1/3)'. After three tries it stops and leaves the work queued for the next cycle. A separate three-second nudge covers the rarer cases where the send worked but the device could not record that it worked. | staff / store manager / franchisee | BUILT + PROVEN, OFF | `sync.js:1782-1802 (the three retries), sync.js:52 _maxRetries 3, sync.js:2531 _scheduleSyncRetry (capped at 5 nudges)` | test/smoke-test.js S-67, S-260 |
| **One operation at a time (the sync lock)** | Sending and fetching are never allowed to run on top of each other on one device — the second one waits or is queued. Without this, two half-finished operations could leave the device's bookmark in the wrong place and skip rows permanently. | invisible to everyone; protects the numbers | BUILT + PROVEN, OFF | `sync.js:48 _syncLock, sync.js:1504 / 1865 (the guards), sync.js:2497 _runSyncCycle, sync.js:2519 _drainSyncQueue` | test/smoke-test.js S-128 (a manual sync that collides with a background one is queued and then runs, rather than silently doing nothing while claiming success) |
| **Only one browser tab does the syncing (leader election)** | If someone has the app open in three tabs, only one of them talks to the cloud. The tabs elect a leader by messaging each other; the leader announces itself every 4 seconds, and if it goes quiet for 10 seconds (tab closed, phone backgrounded) another tab takes over. The other tabs just refresh their screen when the leader brings new data down. | store manager / director (anyone with multiple tabs open) | BUILT + PROVEN, OFF | `sync.js:1038 _initLeaderElection, sync.js:645-646 (10s timeout, 4s heartbeat), sync.js:1191 _tryClaimLeader, sync.js:1216 _becomeLeader` | test/smoke-test.js S-07 (a non-leader tab ignores a background-sync nudge), S-22 (the manual Force Push claims leadership first rather than being a back door), S-124 |
| **Tabs keep each other up to date** | When the syncing tab brings down new data it tells the other tabs, which reload their view. | **Recovery mode if the local database will not open** | If the device's database cannot be opened at all, the app falls back to the last plain-text copy it kept and shows a blocking warning that the numbers shown are an old backup and must not be relied on. It never silently presents stale stock as current. | staff / store manager / director | LIVE | `db.js:1385-1405 (initDB's fallback), db.js:1298 _migrateFromLocalStorage (a failed upgrade throws rather than booting empty)` | test/smoke-test.js S-54 (a failed upgrade throws so the app serves the intact old data instead of an empty app) |

<details><summary>Notes and gotchas (31)</summary>

- **Offline-first: the app saves to the device before it saves to the cloud** — The local database is IndexedDB via Dexie. Reference lists (products, stores, users) are rewritten whole on each save because they are small; movement rows are added one at a time so it stays fast as the ledger grows.
- **'You are offline' red bar** — It reacts to the browser's own online/offline flag, which reports 'online' when the wifi is joined but the internet is actually dead. So the bar can say you are online when sync is in fact failing. The 'Retry' button on the bar just reloads the page.
- **Automatic background sync (save → 0.8s pause → send; plus a check every 30 seconds)** — OFF on real devices because sync never starts at all — see 'Cloud config channel'. Sync.init() bails at sync.js:2596-2603 and shows 'Cloud sync unavailable — config not found. Contact Kunal.'
- **'Sync now' button and the 'Stock last synced' label** — The button exists and is clickable today, but with no cloud config it does nothing. The label will read 'Stock last synced: never' forever on the live site.
- **Auto-sync the moment the internet comes back** — Same blocker: no config, no sync.
- **Sending work up (push) with an honest receipt from the server** — Rows the server permanently rejects get flagged in the local database so they stop re-sending forever. The code comments say they are 'surfaced to an admin' — there is NO screen anywhere that lists them. Only a passing warning toast and the diagnostics log.
- **Junk never leaves the device (egress check)** — Client-side only; the server is the real gate. Excluded rows are permanently marked so they cannot block later clean-ups.
- **Fetching other stores' work down (pull), paged safely** — The row-number bookmark replaced an older 'since this time' approach that broke past roughly 5,000 rows — that would have hit any new or long-offline device. The live site still runs the old broken-at-scale version (git show main:sync.js:923 uses $skip paging).
- **Bad data arriving from the cloud is quarantined, not swallowed** — Quarantined rows go to the console and the diagnostics log. There is no screen showing them.
- **Deletes travel between devices (tombstones), including deletes made offline** — Deleting a movement that has already been archived into the cloud archive is blocked outright (S-210).
- **The offline queue survives closing the app** — Nothing on screen tells a user how many changes are waiting. If sync is broken, the queue grows silently.
- **Retry with a growing wait, so a flaky connection heals itself** — A 'not authorised' failure deliberately does NOT retry — see the 401 pause below.
- **Only one browser tab does the syncing (leader election)** — IMPORTANT: leader election is only started AFTER cloud config loads (sync.js:2609). Because config never loads, there is no leader election on the live site at all — so tabs also never refresh each other's screens.
- **Tabs keep each other up to date** — Same blocker as leader election.
- **'Not authorised' pause — no retry storm** — Server-phase feature; not on the live branch at all.
- **Device sync keys (Settings → Cloud Sync → Sync Keys)** — Server-phase only. Not on the live site.
- **Cloud config channel — the app's self-setup line** — THIS HAS NEVER RUN ON ANY DEVICE. The app ships the literal text %%CONFIG_URL%% where the address should be — a placeholder that was supposed to be filled in at deploy time, and no deploy step ever did. .github/workflows/deploy-swa.yml just uploads the repo as-is (skip_app_build: true), no substitution anywhere. sync.js:691 sees the %% and returns immediately. Everything downstream — master data, access policy, franchise pricing, opening snapshot, and the push/pull addresses themselves — has therefore never been delivered.
- **Manual cloud address entry, Test Connection, Force Push Now (Settings → Cloud Sync)** — This is the ONLY route by which the live app could sync today — and it is fragile: the pasted addresses go into session storage, so they are wiped the moment the tab or browser is closed and must be re-pasted every session. The on-screen setup guide still describes an old two-flow SharePoint-file design that does not match what the server actually does now.
- **Record-steps sync — transfers, deliveries and stock takes travel too** — Server-phase only. On the LIVE app, transfers/deliveries/stock takes do not sync between devices at all — only stock levels and the catalogue were ever intended to. The sidebar label deliberately says 'Stock last synced', not 'Last synced', for exactly this reason.
- **Store-scope reconcile — a device only holds data for its own stores** — Server-phase only. The push-before-wipe order was a real data-loss bug found and fixed during the org chunk.
- **'Store update in progress' hold** — Server-phase only.
- **Head-office cost prices fetched separately (and only to head-office devices)** — Runs off the config fetch, so it has never run.
- **On-demand archive fetch for old movements** — Server-phase only.
- **Offline app shell — the app opens with no internet** — Network-first means that on a slow-but-alive wifi, every file waits for the network to time out before the stored copy is used — the app can feel very slow on bad wifi rather than instantly falling back. Cloud calls are deliberately never cached. The live site runs an older cache version (bob-stock-v10) that still pulls one library from an outside CDN; the working branch hosts everything itself.
- **'A new version is ready — refresh to update'** — If a user never refreshes, they keep running the old version indefinitely.
- **Install to the home screen (add the app to a phone/tablet)** — The banner only appears once someone is logged in, and only in browsers that offer the install prompt — on iPhone/iPad Safari there is no such prompt, so staff must use Share → Add to Home Screen manually and will never see this banner.
- **Background sync and push-notification hooks** — Background sync only exists on Chrome-family browsers; it does nothing on iPhone/iPad. The push-notification code is dead — nothing anywhere sends a push, no subscription is ever created, and no permission is ever requested.
- **Ask the browser not to throw the data away** — It is a request, not a guarantee — the browser can refuse, and the app does not check the answer or warn anyone if it is refused.
- **Manual backup: Download Backup (Settings → Data & Backup)** — IMPORTANT DIFFERENCE between the live site and the working branch. LIVE (main): the export is NOT password-confirmed and it still INCLUDES staff password and PIN hashes — the toast even says so. Working branch: it asks for the Director's password first, and strips password/PIN hashes, head-office cost when not permitted, and the access policy. Until the working branch ships, a downloaded backup file is a sensitive document.
- **Restore from a backup file, with an undo** — Two limits worth knowing: the file must be under 5 MB, and the safety snapshot is kept in browser storage which is usually 5-10 MB total — so on a device with a long history a restore can refuse with 'could not save a safety snapshot first'. Also, the restore works by writing the file into the OLD storage location and reloading, letting the app's one-time upgrade routine import it (db.js:1298). It works, but it is indirect and would break if that upgrade routine were ever removed.
- **Diagnostics log and download** — The version stamp inside the diagnostics file is hard-coded to 'bob-stock-v10' (index.html:6311) while the app's actual cache version is v14 — so a support file will report the wrong version.

</details>

<details><summary>Notes and gotchas (34)</summary>

- **The app's own database on each phone or PC** — VERIFIED TODAY by downloading db.js from the live site: it is byte-identical to branch 'main' (1064 lines) and contains none of the newer server-phase features. The working branch adds a 14th table (recordSteps) that is NOT on the live site.
- **Database upgrades that don't lose data** — Version 1 is live. Version 2 is built but not shipped. The upgrade itself has never been rehearsed on a device that already holds data — worth one deliberate test before the branch ships.
- **First-run starter data (the seeded product and store list)** — The seeded store list is now 9 stores and matches the business. Cockburn's franchise discount is still seeded as a single flat 25 rather than the Retail 50% / everything-else 25% split Kunal decided on 2026-08-02, so any device set up fresh today still gets the wrong discount. Ardross and Bunbury were DELETED from the seed on 2026-08-02 (33 practice movements, 12 threshold entries and both store rows removed); test/check-seed-integrity.js now fails if either reappears.
- **One-time move from the old storage format** — This same code path is what makes 'Restore from backup' work: restore writes the backup into the old-format key and reloads, and this migration picks it up. So it is not really legacy — it is load-bearing for restore, and it is untested.
- **Everything held in memory for instant screens** — The trade-off: memory and disk can briefly disagree. Most of db.js exists to make sure memory is rolled back whenever a disk write fails, so the screen never shows something that wasn't actually saved.
- **Automatic retry when a save fails** — Four attempts total, at 0 / 0.5s / 1s / 2s.
- **Full-screen 'SAVE FAILED — DO NOT CONTINUE' stop screen** — Also used at startup if the database cannot be opened at all (index.html:6336) and in recovery mode.
- **'Data is still saving' warning when closing the tab** — Modern browsers show their own generic wording, not ours, and some ignore it entirely on mobile. Treat it as a courtesy, not a guarantee.
- **Nothing is confirmed on screen until it is really saved** — This is the single most important data-safety property in the app. It was added after an audit found the app was reporting success on writes that had not landed.
- **All-or-nothing transfer save** — If it fails, the transfer object is restored from a snapshot the caller took before touching it.
- **All-or-nothing delivery save** — On failure it reloads the whole cache from disk rather than unpicking individual changes — safe but slow.
- **Deleting a movement leaves a permanent record** — Works offline: a delete made with no internet still leaves a durable record queued for the next sync.
- **Old delete-records tidied away after 30 days** — Only prunes records already confirmed as synced — an unsynced delete is never touched.
- **Ticking off what has reached the cloud** — This replaced an earlier version that rewrote all 12 tables on every successful push — a real performance bug found in audit.
- **Rows the cloud permanently refuses are parked, not retried forever** — NOT on the live site — proven absent from the deployed db.js today. The cloud half (push-v2 ingest validation) is listed in AZURE-CHUNK-AA-LA-CHANGES.md §3 as still to be applied to staging, so I could not confirm the server actually returns rejections yet.
- **The transfer / delivery / stock-take step diary** — NOT on the live site — the deployed database has no recordSteps table at all. Its cloud endpoints exist only on staging.
- **Shrinking the device once Head Office archives old history** — Doubly off. The code is not on the live site, AND the summary is delivered through the cloud config channel that has never worked (sync.js ships CONFIG_URL as an unsubstituted placeholder). No device has ever received a snapshot.
- **Clearing out a store's data when a device no longer covers it** — NOT on the live site. The whole read-diff-write runs as one locked operation so a second browser tab writing at the same moment cannot be wiped out mid-purge.
- **The clean-out refuses to run if anything hasn't reached the cloud** — Deliberately belt-and-braces: the caller already flushes pending work first. Cost history is never touched by the clean-out because it belongs to the product, not the store.
- **Locking a device that writes to a store it no longer owns** — NOT on the live site. Note the honest framing in the code: the stored list of your stores is a cache, never a guarantee — the real enforcement has to be the cloud.
- **Cleaning risky characters out of names and IDs** — GOTCHA FOR TRAINING: this silently removes apostrophes. A store or supplier called "Kunal's" will be stored as "Kunals". Nobody is told; the character just disappears.
- **A product with no on/off setting counts as ON** — Both the seed loader and the every-load normaliser go through one shared function so the test can break a single point and prove the check bites.
- **Rows with dangerous IDs are hidden from the screen** — Because it is memory-only, the bad rows reload from disk on every start and are re-filtered each time. That is deliberate.
- **Download a full backup (Settings → Data & Backup)** — Requires a password re-prompt first. Export is deliberately blocked while a store-scope or policy clean-out is still pending, so a backup can never smuggle out data the device was told to drop. The file still contains full internal business data — it must be handled as sensitive.
- **Restore from a backup file, with a gauntlet of checks** — Max file size 5MB. Restore works by writing the backup into the old-format storage key and reloading — the startup migration then rebuilds the real database from it. That means a large restore can hit the browser's ~5MB storage-key limit before the database limit.
- **Undo the last restore** — Only one level of undo, and only the most recent restore. The undo button only appears when a snapshot exists.
- **Backup file tamper / corruption check** — It detects accidental corruption and casual editing. It is not a security seal — anyone who edits the file can recompute the fingerprint.
- **The running stock number (how 'on hand' is worked out)** — Quantities that are negative, fractional, absurdly large or not a number are skipped rather than silently coerced — a tampered row cannot poison the total.
- **Stock-cache health check** — SURPRISE: nothing in the app ever calls it. It can only be run by a developer typing into the browser console. Grep confirms zero callers outside the test suite. It also quietly modifies the cache when it runs, so it is not purely a read-only check.
- **Disaster recovery if the database won't open** — The fallback copy is whatever was there at the last migration or restore — it could be months old. The warning is deliberately alarming for that reason. Untested.
- **Ask the browser not to throw our data away** — It is a request, not a guarantee — the browser can refuse, and it is fire-and-forget so we never find out whether it was granted. There is no low-storage warning anywhere in the app.
- **Wipe the local database** — SURPRISE: the code comment says 'only called from settings', but grep finds zero callers anywhere. There is no way to trigger it from the app. To clear a device today you clear the browser's site data by hand.
- **Device-only by default — nothing leaves unless sync is set up** — CRITICAL FOR TRAINING. Combined with the fact that the app has never been used, treat every device today as holding data that exists nowhere else. Exporting a backup file is currently the only reliable way to get data off a device.
- **What survives a reinstall, and what does not** — There is no warning anywhere in the app about this, and no reminder to take a backup. For alpha this is the single biggest data-loss risk: a staff member 'clearing their browser' to fix a slow phone destroys that store's history.

</details>

## <a id="cloud-functions"></a>The cloud brain (Azure Functions)

| Feature | What it does | Who | Status | Where | Proven by |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Store device key check (validateKeys)** | When a tablet in a salon tries to send or fetch stock data, the cloud first checks the secret key that device was set up with. It answers a plain yes/no for the store's own key and for the Head Office 'Director' key. It never tells the caller anything else — no hints an attacker could use to guess a key. If the checker is broken or unreachable, the answer is treated as NO, so a broken check locks people out rather than letting them in. | every device — staff, store manager, franchisee, Head Office. Set up once per store by a director. | BUILT, OFF | `azure-functions/src/functions/validateKeys.js:73 (route), :57 (the yes/no decision), :29 (max 20 key rows per call)` | NONE as an automated test. Proven ONCE against the real deployed route by audit-artifacts/rekey-staging.js on 2026-07-22 — all six staging store keys verified true, plus a deliberate wrong-key rejection. |
| **Person login check + 12-hour session pass (validateUser)** | Checks a person's username and password against the staff list held in the cloud, and if correct hands back a short-lived digital 'pass'. A normal login pass lasts 12 hours. For anything risky — publishing prices, archiving, adding users, approving a correction — it instead issues a single-use pass that lasts only 5 minutes and only works for that one job. Locked or deactivated accounts are refused, and a made-up username takes exactly as long to reject as a real one, so nobody can fish for valid usernames. | staff, store manager, franchisee, director, head office — anyone who logs in by name | BUILT + PROVEN, OFF | `azure-functions/src/functions/validateUser.js:168 (route), :84 (the check), :74 (pass minting), :30 (the list of 12 risky jobs)` | test/access-policy-proof.js — 67 PASS (run today). Also proven live: audit-artifacts/mint-test-director.js, 10/10 against the deployed route, 2026-07-23. |
| **Pass checker (verifyProof)** | Every later request carries the pass instead of the password. This route confirms the pass is genuine, has not expired, was issued for the job being attempted, and was issued to this device. It means a password only ever travels at login time, never again. | all logged-in roles, invisibly | BUILT + PROVEN, OFF | `azure-functions/src/functions/validateUser.js:169 (route); pass format documented at :16-19` | test/access-policy-proof.js — 67 PASS (run today); live proof in audit-artifacts/mint-test-director.js |
| **Create a login / set a password (mintUserCredential)** | When Head Office creates a new staff login or resets a password, this route does the password scrambling on the server rather than on a laptop. It enforces a minimum password length (10 characters for a director, 8 for everyone else) and hands back the scrambled value to be stored. | director / head office (user admin only) | BUILT, OFF | `azure-functions/src/functions/validateUser.js:157-166, route at :170` | NONE as an automated test. Proven live once by audit-artifacts/mint-test-director.js (10/10, 2026-07-23) which minted the staging test director through it. |
| **The one permission decision point (evaluateAccess)** | The single place the cloud decides 'is this person allowed to do this?'. It works through the rules in a fixed order: a specific permission set for that individual person wins outright; otherwise a temporary 24-hour PIN can unlock stock-take counting and transfer-receiving only; otherwise the default for their role applies. If no rulebook has been published, or the request is malformed, the answer is NO. The permission tickboxes inside the app are only a preview of this — this is the real gate. | every role; the rules themselves are set by the director | BUILT + PROVEN, OFF | `azure-functions/src/functions/accessPolicy.js:248 (route), :55-72 (the resolver), :74+ (which jobs need a fresh password)` | test/access-policy-proof.js — 67 PASS (run today). A parity sentinel (S-247) in test/smoke-test.js loads this real file to prove the in-app version agrees with it. |
| **Publish the permission rulebook (policyMerge)** | When the director changes who can do what and hits publish, this route checks the new rulebook before it goes out. It refuses a rulebook that would delete the director role, refuses one that would lock directors out of editing permissions ever again, and refuses one that would downgrade the two protected jobs (editing permissions, managing users) from 'must re-enter your password' to anything weaker. It also stamps the version number itself so a device can never fake one, and blocks a save if someone else published in the meantime. | director / head office | BUILT + PROVEN, OFF | `azure-functions/src/functions/accessPolicy.js:137-197, route at :249` | test/access-policy-proof.js — 67 PASS (run today) |
| **24-hour stock-take PIN (validatePin)** | The director can set a short PIN that lets ordinary staff count stock and receive transfers for up to 24 hours without a manager present. This route checks the PIN and issues a pass tied to that specific account and device, expiring when the PIN does. Clearing the PIN is an instant kill switch — every pass already handed out stops working immediately, but ordinary permission edits do not disturb a PIN unlock in progress. | staff (uses it), director (sets and clears it) | BUILT + PROVEN, OFF | `azure-functions/src/functions/accessPolicy.js:199-232, route at :250; the instant-kill counter at :172-177` | test/access-policy-proof.js — 67 PASS (run today), including 'someone else's pin grant is not yours' |
| **Tamper seal on every stock movement (attestRows)** | Every stock movement — a sale, a transfer, an adjustment — gets a cryptographic wax seal stamped by the server the moment it arrives. The seal covers 19 details: which store, which product, how many, the date and time, the reason, where it came from and went to, and the pricing that applied. If anyone later edits any of those directly in the underlying spreadsheet, the seal breaks and the money engine can see it. If the sealing service is down, the whole batch is refused and the device retries — a movement is never allowed to land unsealed. | every device pushing stock movements; the benefit lands with the director and franchisee at settlement time | BUILT + PROVEN, OFF | `azure-functions/src/functions/attestRows.js:257 (route), :69-74 (the 19 sealed details), :80+ (how the seal is computed), :64 (batch limit 5000 rows)` | test/attest-proof.js — 58/58 PASS (run today). Proven on the real cloud 13/13 on 2026-07-23, including an actual spreadsheet-level quantity edit that broke the seal. |
| **Extra seal types for corrections and archiving (attestRows new frames)** | Four additional kinds of wax seal, built to support the correction feature: one for a correction record itself, one for the archive 'era' marker, and two others. They stamp different sets of details but work the same way. | director (corrections), head office (archiving) | BUILT + PROVEN, OFF | `azure-functions/src/functions/attestRows.js:125-256 (FRAME_FIELDS at :148, epoch-v1 at :156, frame dispatch at :244-249)` | test/correction-proof.js — 200/200 PASS (run today), including F30-F32 seal round-trip probes |
| **Franchise buy-back settlement engine (buybackExport)** | When Head Office buys a store back from a franchisee, this works out exactly what is owed. It walks every stock movement in the window, applies the franchise discount that was in force on each individual date, and produces a line-by-line bill plus totals, a usage tally, and a separate informational retail-profit view. Crucially it will NOT sign off a final number it cannot stand behind: it either refuses outright (something is corrupt), marks the result PROVISIONAL and names exactly what is blocking it, or declares it FINAL. There is no 'quietly a bit wrong' outcome. | director and head office (running the settlement); the franchisee receives the result | BUILT + PROVEN, OFF | `azure-functions/src/functions/buybackExport.js:885 (route), buildBuybackExport; the bill lines at :772; the totals, usage and retail-profit block at :855-868` | test/buyback-export-proof.js — 170/170 PASS (run today). Client/engine agreement sentinels S-283..S-286 in test/smoke-test.js load this real engine. |
| **Product and store list publishing (catalogueMerge)** | When Head Office adds a product, renames a category, changes a price or adds a store, this route checks and merges the change before it goes out to every device. It rejects prices that are negative, absurdly large or have more than two decimals, rejects a franchise discount over 100%, rejects a product pointing at a category that does not exist, and rejects a store type that is not one of inline, kiosk, franchise, online or warehouse. It detects two people editing the same row at once, and it stamps the version number itself. | head office / director | BUILT, OFF | `azure-functions/src/functions/catalogueMerge.js:172 (route), :48-68 (the checks), :70+ (the cost strip)` | NONE. No test file references this route at all. |
| **Correction and deletion brain (correctionCompute)** | The decision engine behind a director correcting or deleting a stock movement after the fact — working out what the corrected quantity should be, minting the replacement record, deciding whether a correction can legally be made in the current state, and making sure the same correction submitted twice does not apply twice. It is a pure calculator: it never touches the data itself, it just decides. | director (approving a correction); head office | BUILT + PROVEN, OFF | `azure-functions/src/functions/correctionCompute.js:824 (route), :803-821 (the 18 decision jobs it answers)` | test/correction-proof.js — 200/200 PASS (run today); contract harness audit-artifacts/check-fn-contracts.js |
| **Archive the old ledger safely (snapshotCompute)** | The stock ledger cannot grow forever, so old movements get moved to an archive. Before anything is moved, this route calculates the opening balance to carry forward and then PROVES the maths adds up: for every store-and-product combination, opening balance plus what is left must equal what was there before. If a single pair is off by one, it returns a refusal and nothing is archived — the live ledger is left untouched. | head office / director (runs the archive); protects everybody's stock counts | BUILT + PROVEN, OFF | `azure-functions/src/functions/snapshotCompute.js:322 (route), :140 (the calculation), :293 (the NOT_NEUTRAL refusal), :97 (fidelity hashing)` | test/archive-carry-proof.js — 28/28 PASS (run today). Proven live 17/17 on 2026-07-23 with a real archive run against a dedicated probe list. |
| **Ownership change planner (topologyPlan)** | The single brain behind the store wizard. Give it one instruction — create a store, onboard a brand new franchisee, add another store to an existing franchisee, convert a Head Office store to a franchise, or buy a franchise back — and it works out everything that must happen: who owns the store from when, which franchise rate starts, which logins gain or lose access to that store, which personal staff logins get cancelled, whether an opening stock snapshot is needed, and whether a buy-back settlement must run. It refuses anything that does not add up, such as converting a store to a franchisee whose office login has been deactivated. | director / head office only | BUILT + PROVEN, OFF | `azure-functions/src/functions/topology.js:663 (route); the five instructions at :540 (create HO), :548 (create franchise / add store), :570 (onboard), :618 (convert), :633 (buy-back)` | test/topology-proof.js — 256 PASS (run today) |
| **Who owned it, and at what rate, on that date (topologyResolve)** | A lookup the reports use: for any date, who owned this store then, and what franchise discount applied to this product then. Rates are kept as a dated history that is only ever added to, so a report about last March uses last March's rate even if the rate changed since. The discount can be set as one rate for the whole store, with a per-product rate overriding it for specific items. | franchisee and head office, through reports and the buy-back settlement | BUILT + PROVEN, OFF | `azure-functions/src/functions/topology.js:664 (route); resolvePricingForProduct at :149-173; store default key '*' at :148` | test/topology-proof.js — 256 PASS (run today). Client/server agreement sentinel S-261 in test/smoke-test.js uses these real functions. |
| **Delivery money checker (validateMoney)** | When a delivery is recorded with costs — freight, tax, shipping, customs, unit costs, a foreign-currency rate — this route checks every money figure before it is saved: it must be a real number, not negative, no more than 10 million, and at most two decimal places. Any delivery step that fails comes back flagged and is rejected. | store manager and head office recording deliveries | BUILT, OFF | `azure-functions/src/functions/validateMoney.js:49 (route), :26-47 (the delivery checks), :14 (the money rule)` | NONE. No test file references this route. |
| **Every route is locked behind a key** | None of the 15 routes can be called by an anonymous stranger on the internet. Each one requires a secret function key that only the Head Office workflows hold. There is no public endpoint anyone could poke at to guess passwords or fish for data. | protects everybody; invisible | BUILT, OFF | `authLevel:'function' on all 15 registrations — validateKeys.js:75, validateMoney.js:53, catalogueMerge.js:174, accessPolicy.js:248-250, validateUser.js:168-170, attestRows.js:259, buybackExport.js:886, correctionCompute.js:826, snapshotCompute.js:324, topology.js:663-664` | verified today by reading every registration; the deployed route list was confirmed via az functionapp function list |
| **Broken security service = locked out, not let in** | If the secret used to check passwords or seals is missing or misconfigured, the route returns an error rather than a yes — and the calling workflow treats any error as a refusal. So a misconfiguration or an outage stops work; it never silently waves people through. The one deliberate exception is the delivery money checker, because the app double-checks that itself. | protects everybody; invisible | BUILT + PROVEN, OFF | `accessPolicy.js:234-246 and validateUser.js:140-152 (the shared wrapper), validateKeys.js:79-83, attestRows.js:38-42 (the fail-closed contract)` | test/access-policy-proof.js (67 PASS) and test/attest-proof.js (58/58); proven live in the 2026-07-23 outage drill (P5) where a broken key ring made the whole batch retryable and nothing was inserted |
| **Anti-guessing protections** | Password and PIN checks always take the same amount of work whether the username exists or not, and always return the same unhelpful answer, so nobody can tell a real login from a fake one by timing or wording. Comparisons are done in a way that cannot be probed character by character. Seal-verification answers are a bare yes/no with no detail attached. | protects everybody; invisible | BUILT + PROVEN, OFF | `validateUser.js:91-92 and :20-24 (anti-enumeration), accessPolicy.js:207 (PIN work burn), validateKeys.js:50-55 (constant-time compare), attestRows.js:34 (booleans only, no oracle)` | test/access-policy-proof.js — 67 PASS (run today) |
| **Request size limits** | Each route caps how much it will accept in one go, so a single bad or malicious request cannot tie the cloud up. Login-type routes take at most 50 records; the key checker at most 20; the sealing route takes up to 5,000 stock movements because a tablet that has been offline for weeks pushes its whole backlog at once. | protects everybody; invisible | BUILT, OFF | `accessPolicy.js:243, validateUser.js:149 (50 rows), validateKeys.js:29 (20 rows), attestRows.js:64 (5000 rows)` | test/attest-proof.js — 58/58 PASS covers the seal batch behaviour |
| **Seal key rotation (key ring)** | The secret used to stamp tamper seals can be replaced without invalidating anything already sealed. Each seal records which key stamped it, so old seals keep verifying against the old key while new movements are stamped with the new one. Retiring a key deliberately invalidates its seals, which fails safe. | head office / whoever maintains the system | BUILT + PROVEN, OFF | `azure-functions/src/functions/attestRows.js:52-60 (the key ring contract), :77-78 (kid format)` | test/attest-proof.js — 58/58 PASS (run today) |
| **Cost price never leaves the server for ordinary devices** | What Head Office paid for a product is stripped out of the product list before it is sent to any device, and travels only in a separately locked file that a permission check guards. A salon tablet physically does not have the buy-in cost on it. | protects head office and franchisee commercial data; affects what staff and store managers can see | BUILT, OFF | `azure-functions/src/functions/catalogueMerge.js:70+ (publicRow), :16-18 (the D-COST rule)` | NONE at the route level (no catalogueMerge test); the client-side half is covered by sentinel S-248 in test/smoke-test.js |
| **Refuse, provisional or final — never a quietly wrong number** | The money-critical routes are built so there are only three possible outcomes: a flat refusal because something is corrupt, a result marked PROVISIONAL with every blocking reason named, or a genuinely FINAL result. There is no fourth outcome where a number comes out looking finished but is silently short or mis-valued. | director, head office and franchisee — this is the trust model behind any settlement figure | BUILT + PROVEN, OFF | `buybackExport.js:14-24 (the three outcome classes); snapshotCompute.js:293 (NOT_NEUTRAL refusal); correctionCompute.js:57 (typed refusals)` | test/buyback-export-proof.js 170/170, test/correction-proof.js 200/200, test/archive-carry-proof.js 28/28 (all run today) |

<details><summary>Notes and gotchas (23)</summary>

- **Store device key check (validateKeys)** — Supports key rotation: a store can have an old and a new key both working until a 'grace' date passes. There is no automated test file for this route at all — the only evidence is a one-off cloud run nine days ago. The live app does not call it.
- **Person login check + 12-hour session pass (validateUser)** — The cloud copy is STALE. The Function App was last deployed 2026-07-23; this file changed 2026-07-25 to add the 'correction' job type. So the deployed version cannot issue a correction pass. A throwaway test director account (srvaudit_director) still exists in staging and must be deleted before go-live.
- **Pass checker (verifyProof)** — It also accepts the special 'PIN grant' pass minted by validatePin, but deliberately will not let a password login mint one — a store account can never talk itself into the Director's PIN.
- **Create a login / set a password (mintUserCredential)** — Worth knowing: this route hands the scrambled password back to its caller. The design comment on the older key system (validateKeys.js:22) still says 'There is NO hash-emitting route anywhere' — that statement is now out of date. It is safe in practice because the route is key-locked and only a Logic App can call it, but the doc and the code disagree.
- **The one permission decision point (evaluateAccess)** — Wired into one staging Logic App (access-policy-write-staging) only. The seven other places it was meant to guard — pushing stock, receiving steps, cost visibility, archive access, user admin, catalogue editing, PIN grants — are all still on the 'not applied' list.
- **Publish the permission rulebook (policyMerge)** — Known soft fault (tracked as SRV-P3, fails safe): the calling Logic App writes three separate items one after another. If it dies halfway the director can be temporarily blocked from republishing until it is reconciled. No bad rulebook can ship — it just jams.
- **24-hour stock-take PIN (validatePin)** — The route is deployed but NOTHING CALLS IT. The Logic App change that would route a PIN entry here (user-verify, item 10) has never been applied, so today a PIN would still be checked on the device.
- **Tamper seal on every stock movement (attestRows)** — The core sealing (econ-v1) is deployed and proven end-to-end on staging. But the file changed again on 2026-07-28 to add four MORE seal types, and the Function App has not been redeployed since 2026-07-23 — so those additions are not in the cloud. Also uses a key ring so a compromised key can be rotated without invalidating old seals.
- **Extra seal types for corrections and archiving (attestRows new frames)** — Committed 2026-07-28, five days after the last cloud deploy. Not in the cloud. Part of the parked Contract 2 work.
- **Franchise buy-back settlement engine (buybackExport)** — The route is deployed but NO Logic App calls it — there is no buy-back export workflow in the resource group at all. Retail profit is deliberately marked 'informational only' because sale prices come from the device and have no server proof; the payable is supply cost only. The engine is FROZEN pending a return audit — three known money-critical checks are still owed to it.
- **Product and store list publishing (catalogueMerge)** — Important privacy behaviour: the buy-in cost price is STRIPPED out of every product row before it goes to devices, and travels only in a separately locked cost file. Wired to bob-stock-catalogue-write-staging. This is the biggest untested route in the area — it decides what every device sees.
- **Correction and deletion brain (correctionCompute)** — THIS IS THE ONE MISSING ROUTE — the repo registers 15, the cloud has 14, and this is the gap. Deliberately parked on 2026-07-29 (audit-artifacts/C2-PARKING-DECISION.md). It also carries a known defect: the record IDs it mints contain a colon (e.g. 'ctl:op-0001') which the settlement engine's ID rule rejects, so if deployed today it would abort a whole store's buy-back export. Must be fixed before it ships.
- **Archive the old ledger safely (snapshotCompute)** — Cloud copy is STALE — the file changed 2026-07-26 (typed hashing v2 and a new control-manifest requirement) after the 2026-07-23 deploy. The live run found a real latent bug that would have hit production: the archive column stored time as text while the source stored it as a number, so every row was rejected. Fixed. The cutoff is by row ID, not by date, so a backdated movement entered late is never lost.
- **Ownership change planner (topologyPlan)** — Deployed but NOTHING CALLS IT — there is no topology-change Logic App in the resource group. Two real cases are due around Sep-Oct 2026 (a Head Office store going to a new franchisee, and a new store for an existing franchisee), so this needs its workflow built. The shared store till login is deliberately protected and survives an ownership change; only personal logins are cancelled.
- **Who owned it, and at what rate, on that date (topologyResolve)** — This is the built-and-off era-aware pricing. It is NOT missing. Two gaps against what the owner decided: (1) there is no PRODUCT TYPE tier — only a whole-store rate and a per-product override, so 'Retail 50% / professional 25%' cannot be expressed as a type rule today; (2) nothing writes or delivers a pricing file, so there is no rate history in existence yet. If the pricing data is malformed in any way it returns 'no rate' rather than guessing.
- **Delivery money checker (validateMoney)** — Deliberately the ONLY fail-open route in the whole area: if it is unreachable the delivery is still accepted, because the app itself also checks the same rules. Every other route fails closed. Built because a bug let negative and garbage delivery costs through.
- **Every route is locked behind a key** — A temporary 'auditors' key created for an external audit was deleted afterwards; only the default key remains.
- **Broken security service = locked out, not let in** — Proven for real on the cloud, not just in tests — the outage drill deliberately broke the key ring and confirmed nothing landed.
- **Anti-guessing protections** — Added after an external auditor pointed out the original design leaked whether a username existed.
- **Request size limits** — The 5,000 cap was raised from 500 during the cloud apply after someone realised a long-offline tablet would exceed the old cap forever and retry in a permanent loop.
- **Seal key rotation (key ring)** — Live on staging as BOB_ROW_ATTEST_ACTIVE=k1 with one key configured. Deliberately a different secret from the password one, so a leak of one does not compromise the other.
- **Cost price never leaves the server for ordinary devices** — The permission-based version of this (the seeCost check replacing a simple flag) is item 6 on the not-yet-applied list.
- **Refuse, provisional or final — never a quietly wrong number** — The settlement engine is also clock-free and randomness-free — the same inputs always produce exactly the same answer, so a settlement can be regenerated and compared.

</details>

## <a id="archive-ledger"></a>Archiving and long-term data

| Feature | What it does | Who | Status | Where | Proven by |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Archive run — filing away old stock movements** | Every stock number in the app is worked out by adding up every movement ever recorded. That list grows forever, so eventually phones get slow and the cloud list hits a size limit. The archive run fixes that: it works out the closing balance for every product in every store up to a chosen point, copies the old movements to a separate 'archive' list, and then removes them from the working list. Stock totals stay exactly the same — the app just starts from the balance instead of re-adding a decade of history. | director (only a Director can run it); everyone benefits invisibly | BUILT + PROVEN, OFF | `azure-functions/src/functions/snapshotCompute.js:140-320; the run itself is an Azure Logic App, captured at audit-artifacts/bob-stock-archive-staging-REDACTED-2026-07-31.json (actions: Get_state, Acquire_lock, Read_live, Call_compute, Copy_loop, Verify_content_hash, Publish, Delete_loop, Release_ok)` | test/archive-carry-proof.js (28/28, re-run today); test/smoke-test.js S-202..S-213; a real end-to-end staging run recorded 17/17 in AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-STAGING-LEDGER.md:81 |
| **The stock-neutrality proof (the safety catch on archiving)** | Before anything is archived or deleted, the system proves to itself that the maths still adds up: for every single store-and-product pair, the archived balance plus the movements left behind must equal the old grand total. If even one pair is off, the whole run stops — nothing is published, nothing is copied, nothing is deleted, and the live data is untouched. | director (it protects them); head office | BUILT + PROVEN, OFF | `azure-functions/src/functions/snapshotCompute.js:284-293 (returns ok:false, reason 'NOT_NEUTRAL')` | test/smoke-test.js S-203, S-204; snapshotCompute unit + deployed-parity proofs recorded in AZURE-CHUNK8-WAVE-REVIEW.md |
| **Cutoff by row number, not by date** | The archive boundary is 'everything up to cloud row number N', not 'everything before a date'. That matters because a staff member who logs a movement while offline for a week gets a brand-new, higher row number when it finally uploads. <details><summary>Notes and gotchas (32)</summary>

- **Archive run — filing away old stock movements** — Deployed to the STAGING cloud only, and it has never been run against real data — only against purpose-built probe lists. There is no button in the app: it is fired by an HTTP call from a script that must supply the cutoff itself. Its trigger is 'manual' — no schedule.
- **The stock-neutrality proof (the safety catch on archiving)** — This is the single most important guard in the whole area. It is a hard stop, not a warning.
- **Cutoff by row number, not by date** — This was the crux fix both external auditors independently demanded at design review. The business date is still stored on the row for reporting.
- **Keep-recent window** — The intended window was 'roughly the last 4-8 weeks' (AZURE-CHUNK8-SCOPE.md), but the value is supplied per-run by whoever calls the endpoint. Nothing in the code picks it.
- **Deleted movements stay deleted after archiving** — Known and accepted limitation, written into the code at :162-164: if someone deletes a movement that was archived in an EARLIER run, that old archived balance cannot be corrected retrospectively. The mitigation is the keep-recent window plus a rule of not deleting ancient movements.
- **Copy-fidelity check before anything is deleted** — The June-2026 version of this only fingerprinted a handful of fields. A July upgrade extended it to the full carried set after ground-truthing showed the archive copy had NEVER carried the duplicate-protection key across (AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-STAGING-LEDGER.md:69).
- **Archive keeps the price and supply stamps on the row** — A real latent bug was found during this work: the archive list stored the timestamp as text while the live list stored it as a number, so a genuine archive run from the real list would have failed outright with the run-lock left stuck. Fixed and re-proven (AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-STAGING-LEDGER.md:89-95).
- **One-run-at-a-time lock and safe restart** — During the July archive-carry work a failed insert DID leave the lock stuck on 'running' — the release paths cover refusals, not an unexpected cloud error mid-copy. Worth re-testing.
- **Publish the new balances LAST** — Both external auditors flagged 'no visible partial state' as a must-have at design review.
- **Run size limit — max 5,000 movements per archive run** — The intended cadence (archive at 5,000 rows) was designed to stay under this ceiling, but the cadence itself is not built — so if nobody archives for a long stretch, the first run will simply refuse and someone has to work out how to split it.
- **Devices adopt the published opening balances** — This is the piece the whole feature hangs on and it CANNOT work today: the balances are delivered through the cloud config download, and that channel has never worked (sync.js:64 still ships the build placeholder '%%CONFIG_URL%%' and the fetch bails at sync.js:691). Also the whole client half lives only on the working branch — 'main', which the live site serves, has zero of it.
- **Catch-up safety gate (a device won't use balances it isn't ready for)** — Best single piece of defensive design in this area. Worth explaining to staff as 'the app never guesses'.
- **Balances survive an offline start-up** — Added after an audit found that without it, an offline boot would under-count.
- **Device clean-up — old movements dropped from the phone** — Deliberately never touches an un-uploaded row — that is the no-stock-loss rule.
- **Cloud row numbers stamped on every movement (and back-filled)** — A one-time migration at sync.js:2581-2590 rewinds a device's download pointer to zero so every old row gets a number — a heavy but one-off re-download.
- **You cannot delete or undo an archived movement** — Staff-facing wording already exists and is clear — good training material.
- **Reports warn you when they cannot see far enough back** — Fails safe by design: if the app cannot work out where the boundary is, it warns on EVERY range rather than staying quiet (index.html:1410, 1451).
- **'Load archived data' — pulling old movements back for a report** — TWO REAL DEFECTS, ground-truthed against the deployed definition today. (1) The date range is silently ignored: the app sends from/to at the top level while the Logic App reads them nested under 'data' — so it returns the first 5,000 archived rows in row order regardless of what was asked. (2) The archive read does not ask for the price/supply stamp columns at all, even though the app's reader expects them — so archived rows would come back with their money meaning stripped. Neither is caught by any test, because the tests mock the server.
- **Old transfers and deliveries stay 'completed' after archiving** — An earlier version used a date rule; an auditor pointed out that would falsely mark never-delivered stock as received, so it was rebuilt as a per-item proof.
- **Franchise stores only get their own opening balances** — Fails closed: if the app cannot tell what the account is allowed to see, it allows nothing (index.html:1370-1376).
- **Losing the 'see archived data' permission wipes it from the device** — The client half is built and proven. The server half — the archive read checking the permission rather than just the device type — is written up but NOT applied (AZURE-CHUNK-AA-LA-CHANGES.md §6, listed as outstanding item 7 in AZURE-CHUNK-AA-STAGING-LEDGER.md:55).
- **New/returning device catch-up (the pull-hardening upgrade)** — BUILT IN JUNE 2026, FULLY PROVEN, AND NEVER INSTALLED. The live site still uses the old method — verified today: the 'main' branch sync.js has no trace of the new cursor and still sends the old since/skip request. A ready-to-run cutover procedure exists at AZURE-PULL-HARDENING-CUTOVER-RUNBOOK.md and has never been executed.
- **Catch-up safety rules (frozen ceiling, look-back, stop-on-nonsense)** — The look-back of 100 was argued between the two auditors (one wanted 20, one wanted 1,000+) and settled at 100 with written reasoning at sync.js:1853-1858. Standing rule attached to it: if the upload concurrency is ever raised, raise this first.
- **Old-and-new-at-once download endpoint (safe cutover)** — Built in June specifically because both auditors called the same-instant swap the number-one risk. Never deployed.
- **Standing rule: never edit the movements list directly in SharePoint** — This is a policy, not code — nothing in the system stops a hand edit. It needs to be in the Head Office handbook the day the new catch-up is installed.
- **Buy-back settlement export engine** — The calculation is deployed on the staging Function App (it went live as a side effect of a July deploy — 14 routes total, AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-STAGING-LEDGER.md:13-19), but NOTHING CALLS IT. The gated route that would feed it real data was never built (AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-LA-CHANGES.md §3), and there is no button anywhere in the app — 'buyback' does not appear once in index.html, sync.js or phase2.js.
- **Settlement verdicts: FINAL, PROVISIONAL, or refused** — The engine is deliberately clock-free and random-free, so re-running it on the same evidence always produces the identical statement.
- **Retail profit shown but marked 'information only'** — Revenue is already net of customer refunds, per a decision Kunal made on 2026-07-20.
- **Correction-aware archiving (built, deliberately parked)** — Confirmed off today: the deployed archive Logic App definition contains no 'controlHeads' or 'controlProtocol' at all, so the legacy path always runs. This belongs to the parked C2 work (audit-artifacts/C2-PARKING-DECISION.md).
- **Build-mismatch belt on the archive run** — Deliberately derived rather than hand-typed, after an audit round found the first version was a constant somebody had to remember to bump.
- **Archive cadence rule (decided, not built)** — The archive Logic App's trigger is manual HTTP only — verified today in the captured definition. Somebody must decide the cutoff row number and call it. This is the biggest missing piece for making archiving actually operable.
- **Era cutoff — a new franchisee cannot read the old owner's history** — Ground-truthed on 2026-07-30 and re-confirmed today: the ownership-dates data ('store_eras') has never existed anywhere in the code — it appears only in specification documents. Because the current read treats an empty filter as 'no filter', a naive implementation would hand a new franchisee the previous owner's entire trading history.

</details>

| Feature | What it does | Who | Status | Where | Proven by |

| **Account Access screen (Settings → Account Access tab)** | A Director-only screen inside Settings where you decide what each kind of account is allowed to do — instead of those rules being fixed in the app. It shows a grid: every job the app can do down the left, every account type across the top, and a tick box where they meet.
