But it must be fixed BEFORE the cloud doors start reading the permission list (ITEM5-RESPEC:415). | Claude, confirmed by both audits 2026-07-09 | `AZURE-CHUNK-AA-STAGING-LEDGER.md:34-45; AZURE-CHUNK-AA-EXTERNAL-AUDIT-RESPONSE.md:13-16` |
| Item 5 two-stage design | The two doors every stock movement passes through get their new machinery installed SWITCHED OFF first (Stage 1, proven to change nothing), and turned on later by creating a single row of data (Stage 2). While off, anything unexpected — missing row, failed lookup, wrong list name — counts as OFF. | These two doors run on the same connection as the LIVE app. The previous version of this plan was reviewed clean three times and was still wrong three ways, because the reviews checked the design while the instructions had been written against a day-old picture of the actual machine. All three would have misbehaved on ordinary everyday traffic with nothing published at all. | Rules out 'unknown means enforce' during Stage 1 (that hardening is deliberately a later, separately audited item); rules out applying these two doors first in the batch; rules out applying both halves in one sitting; rules out flipping with only one door armed. | Claude, folding 3 audit rounds 2026-07-30 / corrected 2026-07-31 | `AZURE-CHUNK-AA-ITEM5-RESPEC.md:192-206, :402-417 (supersedes AZURE-CHUNK-AA-LA-CHANGES.md §3/§4)` |
| Item 5 PARKED | The two ingest doors are PARKED and are applied LAST. Three audit rounds, three blocks, both auditors every time. They are not to be resumed until four specific preconditions are met — no partial credit. | Kunal's call: the earlier work does not depend on it, so it is not worth being the bottleneck. Resuming early is exactly what produced three rounds. The recurring defect was never a missing check — it was claiming something was covered or authoritative before it was. | Rules out resuming on a partial edit set; rules out asserting coverage as an adjective ('authoritative', 'complete') instead of a measured number; rules out an off-switch design where an ABSENT switch row means ON — which is what the previous version actually did. | Kunal 2026-08-01 | `HANDOVER.md:537-566; commit dc85729` |
| Backlog re-sort (AA impact) | Account Access items 5-11 are SECURITY hardening, and security hardening may now follow release. They sit behind data leaks, money/logic defects and feature gaps in the queue. | Kunal's call today: staff are not technically inclined and are not a hostile-user threat, so the browser-to-cloud permission work can wait — but a franchisee seeing another store's figures, or a wrong number, cannot. This security work had been blocking a queue of money and data-leak defects that need none of it. | Rules out resuming the Account Access cloud applies ahead of the Cockburn pricing and Southlands ownership work; does NOT rule out the data-leak parts of this chunk (the franchise history cutoff, SR-6), which are a leak, not hardening. | Kunal 2026-08-01 | `HANDOVER.md:18-26, :65` |
| Accepted residual — cost history | When 'see cost' is taken away, the device's OWN recorded cost history is not wiped — only the central cost figures it was sent. The cloud refuses all further cost lookups. | That history is data the device itself recorded; deleting it would destroy the store's own records to enforce a view setting. | Rules out an auditor calling this a failed revoke. Note the coupling: granting 'see cost' also reveals the franchise-discount and price COLUMNS in the products table (editing them stays behind a separate permission). | Claude, disclosed to auditors 2026-07-09 | `AZURE-CHUNK-AA-WAVE-REVIEW.md:23-25` |
| R2-2 accepted limitation | A device that is completely offline keeps data it already downloaded until it reconnects. The delete-what-you-may-no-longer-see step fires on reconnect; until then the device cannot talk to the cloud at all. | Standard limitation of an app designed to work offline. Recorded openly so no future audit mistakes it for a hole. | Rules out claiming an instant revoke reaches offline devices. | AGY (raised), accepted 2026-07-09 | `AZURE-CHUNK-ACCOUNT-ACCESS-SCOPE.md:15-18` |
| Conservative v1 prompts | The existing password prompts for publishing, user administration and backup keep asking every time, even if the Director relaxes them in the settings. Only the NEW prompts (approve, resolve, delivery) honour a relaxed setting. | Deliberately conservative first version — these are the highest-consequence actions and nobody had asked for them to be relaxed. | Rules out reading the settings screen as fully honoured today — a Director who un-ticks those three will still be prompted. Worth telling Kunal before he uses that screen. | Claude, disclosed 2026-07-09 | `AZURE-CHUNK-AA-WAVE-REVIEW.md:27-28` |
| D-OS-ERA | A store's ownership is recorded as a dated HISTORY of who owned it and when ('eras'), not as a single 'this store changed hands on date X' field. Every change of hands closes the current era and opens a new one. Who is allowed to see which of a store's records is decided purely by which era those records fall into. | A single takeover date breaks the moment a store changes hands twice. Buy a store back and re-franchise it, and the new franchisee would inherit the previous franchisee's and Head Office's trading history. With eras, each owner sees only their own window, and the earlier takeover date simply becomes the first era boundary rather than a special case. | Rules out a single 'takeover cutoff date' field on the store. Rules out deciding visibility from the store's current franchise flags. Rules out any design where a new owner can be shown the previous owner's trading history. | auditor-driven (Codex R2-3), adopted by Kunal at the 2026-07-10 design session 2026-07-09 (proposed) / 2026-07-10 (adopted) | `AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-STRUCTURE.md:48-52 (model) and :257-262 (the reasoning, from the Codex R2-3 spec review)` |
| D-OS-1 | Two real, concrete situations drive this whole chunk and both must work properly: (A) an existing Head Office store becomes a franchise under a brand-new franchisee, and (B) a brand-new store joins a franchisee who already exists. Equal priority. | These are the actual business events coming up, not hypotheticals. Building to real cases keeps the design honest and stops it drifting into generic 'topology management'. | Rules out building only the conversion path, or only the onboarding path. Rules out treating 'add a store to an existing franchisee' as a rare edge case. | Kunal 2026-07-10 | `AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-STRUCTURE.md:11-14` |
| D-OS-2 | When a Head Office store becomes a franchise: the store's own till/POS login STAYS the same (only who owns it changes). Personal logins tied to that store — staff, store manager, territory manager — lose that store. A person left with no stores at all is switched off; a territory manager who still covers other stores just loses this one and keeps working. | The physical shop keeps trading on the same device, so breaking its login would be pointless disruption. But the people are now the franchisee's responsibility, and the franchisee appoints their own. Switching off a territory manager's whole account because ONE of their five stores converted would be plainly wrong. | Rules out cancelling the store's till login on conversion. Rules out blanket-deactivating every account that touched the store. Rules out leaving ex-Head-Office staff logins with access after the store has changed hands. | Kunal (rule), Claude (multi-store nuance, built and audited) 2026-07-10 (nuance settled by 2026-07-12 build) | `AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-STRUCTURE.md:16-22 (decision + the nuance); AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-ENFORCEMENT-MATRIX.md:68 (the rule pinned); azure-functions/src/functions/topology.js:297 (built: deactivate only if zero stores left)` |
| D-OS-3 | There is NO direct 'sell a store from one franchisee to another' path. It is always done in two steps: buy the store back to Head Office first, then hand it to the new franchisee. | The Head Office period sitting between the two franchise periods IS the clean boundary — it guarantees the incoming franchisee can never see the outgoing one's trading history. The ownership-era model then handles the whole thing for free, with no extra machinery. | Rules out building a fifth wizard tool for franchisee-to-franchisee sale. Rules out any code path that re-owns a store whose current owner is not Head Office. | Kunal 2026-07-10 | `AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-STRUCTURE.md:23-25; enforced in code at azure-functions/src/functions/topology.js:552 and :597 (rejects with DIRECT_TRANSFER_FORBIDDEN)` |
| D-OS-4 | When a franchisee's last store leaves them, the app does NOT automatically switch off their office account. The Director switches it off by hand, once stock and money have been settled. | There may still be stock to transfer or accounts to settle after the store has gone. Automatically killing the login mid-settlement would be unhelpful and hard to undo. | Rules out auto-deactivating an emptied office account. Rules out the reverse mistake too — a buy-back must never REACTIVATE an office the Director had already switched off. | Kunal 2026-07-10 | `AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-STRUCTURE.md:26-29; refined by OS-SR-9 at :124-129; built at azure-functions/src/functions/topology.js:290-293 (an already-deactivated office is never silently switched back on)` |
| OS-SR-9 | An office account left switched on after its last store is gone has zero stores, and therefore sees nothing live at all. Settling up is done BEFORE the buy-back, or afterwards from the handover export — never by leaving them able to pull fresh data. | It resolves the apparent contradiction in D-OS-4: 'stays active for settlement' does not mean 'can still see the store's data'. It just means the login still exists until the Director tidies up. | Rules out any 'historical read-only window' access for an ex-franchisee. Rules out settling up by letting them pull data after the buy-back. | auditor-driven (Codex-4), adopted 2026-07-10 | `AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-STRUCTURE.md:124-129 (folded from the Codex R1 spec review)` |
| D-OS-5 | At buy-back, the departing franchisee is given a DATA EXPORT of their period. Their live access ends immediately and their devices wipe. | They have a legitimate accounting and tax need for their own trading records, but no ongoing business reason to see the store live. An export satisfies the first without any of the risk of the second — and it needs no special ongoing-access machinery. | Rules out giving an ex-franchisee a lingering read-only login. Rules out building any 'historical access window' feature. | Kunal 2026-07-09 | `AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-STRUCTURE.md:30-31 and :232-237` |
| D-OS-6 | The buy-back export contains, for each product over the franchisee's ownership period: how much was used, what it cost them, and the retail profitability. Delivered as a CSV bundle, as a compulsory step inside the buy-back wizard. | It is an accounting and tax hand-off, so it must be the computed summary, not the raw movement log. Making it a wizard step means it cannot be forgotten in the rush of a handover. | Rules out handing over the raw ledger. Rules out leaving the export as an optional afterwards-if-they-ask task. | Kunal 2026-07-10 | `AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-STRUCTURE.md:34-38` |
| OS-SR-4 | The buy-back export is generated by the server and is strictly bounded to the franchisee's own closed period — both the start AND the end date. A record dated exactly at the end moment is excluded. | Without a hard upper bound, Head Office's trading after the buy-back would leak into the ex-franchisee's usage, cost and profit figures — a real number on a real settlement, computed wrong. | Rules out a client-side filtered export. Rules out a 'from this date onwards' export with no end date. | auditor-driven (Codex-3), adopted 2026-07-10 | `AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-STRUCTURE.md:103-106; AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-ENFORCEMENT-MATRIX.md:81-83; AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-LA-CHANGES.md:129` |
| W4-SR-9 | The buy-back settlement must be built from live records AND archived records together, and the engine refuses to produce a settlement at all if the records handed to it do not fully cover the period. Likewise an old franchise invoice must say 'older records need loading' rather than quietly printing a short one. | Old records get moved to an archive. A settlement built from an incomplete set would look perfectly normal while being short by whatever was archived — the worst kind of money error, because nobody would spot it. | Rules out generating a settlement from the live table alone. Rules out any report that silently renders a partial invoice when its date range reaches past the local window. | Kunal 2026-07-13 | `AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-W4-SCOPE.md:26 (W4-SR-9, Kunal 2026-07-13)` |
| D-OS-7 | The wizard's list of fields must be built as data, so more fields can be added later without redesigning the screen. It starts with store name, phone and type; franchise stores add the discount and the office login. | Kunal's own words were 'if there's anything else we can add that later'. Hard-coding the fields guarantees a rebuild the first time he wants one more box. | Rules out a hand-coded fixed form. NOTE: this rule governs Wave 5, which is NOT built — if this document is not re-read at wizard build time the rule is simply lost. | Kunal 2026-07-10 | `AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-STRUCTURE.md:39-42` |
| D-OS-8 | Before anything is committed, the wizard shows a plain-English summary of exactly what is about to change — 'moves Booragoon from Head Office to Franchisee X; X sees data from today; Head Office keeps the full history; the store till stays as-is; these 2 manager logins are cancelled' — and the Director confirms before the change fires. | These changes are irreversible in practice and touch several logins at once. A non-technical Director must be able to see, in words, what is going to happen before it happens. | Rules out a silent one-click commit. Rules out a technical/JSON preview. | Kunal 2026-07-10 | `AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-STRUCTURE.md:43-45 and :67-72` |
| OS-SR-1 | A change of ownership is executed by ONE dedicated server job that writes a journal entry first ('pending'), then applies every sub-change one at a time in a way that is safe to repeat, then marks it complete. If it crashes halfway, a sweep resumes it. While a change is pending on a store, reads for that store are blocked rather than served half-changed data. | The underlying storage has no way to make several list updates succeed-or-fail together. Without a journal, a crash halfway through leaves the business in a split state — some logins moved, some not — with no way to know which. Blocking reads during the window is safer than serving a half-migrated picture. | Rules out the wizard making several separate writes from the browser. Rules out best-effort/no-journal application. Rules out serving reads for a store mid-change. | auditor-driven (Codex-1 + AGY-3, converged), adopted 2026-07-10 | `AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-STRUCTURE.md:84-91; AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-LA-CHANGES.md:9-12 and the §1 route spec` |
| OS-SR-2 | The SERVER works out which logins are affected by an ownership change. The wizard's preview is for display only and its list is never trusted. | If the browser told the server which accounts to update, a tampered request could simply leave the outgoing owner off the list — and they would keep their access after the store had changed hands. | Rules out sending a credential list from the client. Rules out treating the preview as the instruction — it is only a display of the server's own plan. | auditor-driven (Codex-2), adopted 2026-07-10 | `AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-STRUCTURE.md:92-95; AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-LA-CHANGES.md:9-12` |
| OS-SR-8 | Ownership is recorded against a permanent franchisee identity, not against their login. Logins point at that identity. | Logins get deactivated, renamed and recreated. If history pointed at the login, deleting and recreating a franchisee's office account would orphan or misattribute their whole trading history. | Rules out using the office account row or username as the owner key. Rules out reusing a username being harmless. | auditor-driven (Codex-5), adopted 2026-07-10 | `AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-STRUCTURE.md:120-123; AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-ENFORCEMENT-MATRIX.md:48-49` |
| OS-SR-6 | A device that belongs to a shop — the till and the store manager login — can only ever see the CURRENT owner's period. It follows whoever owns the store now and can never look back into a previous owner's trading. | The physical device stays in the shop through a handover. Without this rule the incoming franchisee's till would be sitting on the outgoing franchisee's full history. | Rules out shop devices retaining prior-era data after a handover. Rules out treating shop devices like franchisee/Head Office accounts, which do map to specific periods. | auditor-driven (AGY-4), adopted 2026-07-10 | `AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-STRUCTURE.md:113-116; AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-ENFORCEMENT-MATRIX.md:74-77` |
| OS-SR-5 | Before a device wipes data it is no longer entitled to, it must first send up anything it recorded while offline. Nothing unsent is ever deleted — if the send fails, the wipe refuses and retries every cycle rather than losing the work. | Ground-truthed as a REAL live bug, not a theory: the sync code wiped by store, ignoring whether rows had been uploaded yet, so a staff member's offline stock entries could be destroyed the moment a store left their scope. This protects every scope change, not just ownership ones. | Rules out wiping first and syncing later. Rules out a best-effort wipe that proceeds when the upload failed. IMPORTANT: also rules out re-reporting this as an open bug — it is fixed (see stale records). | auditor-driven (AGY-2), ground-truthed and adopted 2026-07-10 (decided) / 2026-07-12 (built) / 2026-07-13 (audited clean) | `AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-STRUCTURE.md:107-112; the bug at AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-ENFORCEMENT-MATRIX.md:27-30; BUILT and audited at sync.js:2200-2203 (flush) and 2205-2215 (refuse-and-retry), build commit 7aa4733` |
| D-OS-F6 | A device must also reset itself when the OWNERSHIP of one of its stores changes, not just when its list of stores changes. | Discovered by reading the live code: the till keeps exactly the same single store through a conversion, so nothing looked different to it and it would have carried the old owner's data straight into the new ownership. The whole era model would have been silently defeated on the most important case. | Rules out relying on the store-list signature alone as the reset trigger. Rules out a single global version number — it must be per store, or a change to one store is masked by another. | Claude (discovery sweep), folded into the build 2026-07-10 (found) / 2026-07-12 (built) | `AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-ENFORCEMENT-MATRIX.md:31-36; AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-STRUCTURE.md build order :178; BUILT at sync.js:2177-2189 (per-store version map) and 2205 (targeted wipe)` |
| OS-SR-7 | The opening stock balance at a handover is calculated BY THE SERVER, at the exact moment the ownership boundary is set, as one operation. | A balance worked out on a device mid-trading-day drifts against whatever is still being pushed up. Then the new owner starts from a number that never existed. | Rules out a client-generated opening balance. Rules out taking the snapshot at any moment other than the era boundary. | auditor-driven (AGY-5), adopted 2026-07-10 | `AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-STRUCTURE.md:117-119` |
| OS-SR-5/7-AMEND | A handover pauses first: the change stays 'pending' and the opening balance stays provisional until the shop's devices have finished sending up their old work, within a bounded waiting period. Anything that still turns up late is QUARANTINED and shown to the Director to settle by hand — never applied silently. | The two earlier rules collided. Accepting late offline rows (OS-SR-5) versus a finalised opening balance (OS-SR-7) meant a pre-handover entry arriving after the balance was struck would land in the old period and leave the new owner with phantom stock. Making it a visible reconciliation item instead is the honest answer. | Rules out silently applying a late pre-handover row. Rules out finalising the opening balance before devices have drained. Note this is an example of the standing lesson: the FIX itself needed its own adversarial review, and that review found the race the fix introduced. | auditor-driven (Codex-1 + AGY-1 converged), adopted 2026-07-10 | `AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-STRUCTURE.md:136-143` |
| OS-SR-10 | That final catch-up upload from a departing owner's device is authorised by a one-time, expiring permission issued by the server. A record's own claimed date is never enough on its own. | Dates on a record come from the device and can be set to anything. 'Accept it if the date falls in the old period' would let an ex-owner back-date entries into a closed period long after their access ended — directly affecting what they owe. | Rules out trusting the record's timestamp as the authorisation. Rules out an open-ended grace period. | auditor-driven (AGY-2), adopted 2026-07-10 | `AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-STRUCTURE.md:144-149` |
| OS-SR-11 | The franchise discount is kept as its own dated history, completely separate from who owned the store. Ownership periods decide WHOSE records you can see; the discount history decides WHAT RATE applies to a given record's date. | A discount can be renegotiated without the store changing hands, so one ownership period may span two different rates — meaning the rate cannot live on the ownership record. This also fixes a live latent bug that exists today: because only a single current rate is stored, changing it retroactively rewrites every past invoice. | Rules out storing the rate on the ownership era record (which is what OS-SR-3 originally said). Rules out reading the store's current discount when pricing a historical line. Rules out one rate per ownership period. | auditor-driven (AGY-3), ground-truthed and adopted 2026-07-10 | `AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-STRUCTURE.md:150-157; the latent bug confirmed at AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-ENFORCEMENT-MATRIX.md:18-22` |
| OS-SR-12 | Discount rates are append-only once anything has been invoiced against them. Changing a rate opens a NEW period from an effective date; it never edits an old one. Correcting a genuine past mistake requires a logged, visible adjustment — not a quiet overwrite. | Without this, a Director fixing a setup error ('that should have been 12%, not 10%') would silently rewrite every invoice already issued under the old rate. Past invoices need the same immutability as the movement ledger itself. | Rules out an edit button on a closed rate period. Rules out back-dating a rate change over an already-invoiced window. The audited adjustment path itself is NOT built — see open questions. | auditor-driven (Codex R3), adopted 2026-07-10 | `AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-STRUCTURE.md:163-169` |

| W4-SR-7 | On every Head-Office-to-franchise supply line, both the selling price and the discount used are frozen onto the record at the moment it is committed. | D-OS-TOOL4 | Buying a store back needs NO cost-boundary machinery. Because stock records only ever store quantities and movements, and money is worked out at the moment you look at a report, buying a store back simply means the franchise loading stops applying from that point. Nothing is stored, so nothing needs resetting. | Kunal spotted this and it was verified against the code. An auditor had raised 'cost contamination' as a concern, but that concern assumed franchise pricing was baked into stored records — which this app has never done. | Rules out a cost snapshot or reset mechanism at buy-back. Rules out baking franchise prices into stored records. NOTE: this does NOT remove the visibility curtain — the era records still control whose history a franchisee may see. | Kunal (insight), ground-truthed against the code and adopted 2026-07-09 | `AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-STRUCTURE.md:239-251` |
| D10-9 | When a company store becomes a franchise, the new franchisee's cost visibility starts at an opening balance on the takeover date. They do not get the store's full corporate cost history. | The pre-takeover cost history is Head Office's commercial information and has nothing to do with the incoming franchisee's trading. Directors and Head Office keep full visibility throughout — this is a curtain on one side, not a deletion. | Rules out a full-history re-pull on a takeover. Rules out treating a takeover like an ordinary widening of someone's store list. Rules out any suggestion that Head Office loses history. | Kunal 2026-07-08 | `AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-STRUCTURE.md:270-274 (carried in); AZURE-CHUNK10-SCOPE.md §D10-9; the era boundary IS this cutoff — AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-ENFORCEMENT-MATRIX.md:68` |
| W3-SCOPE | Wave 3 builds only the DEVICE half of the store-ownership safety work. The matching cloud changes (the switch that pauses a store during an ownership change, the one-off permission to flush old-era data, the quarantine of late arrivals) are deliberately left to the later staging-apply phase, and the wizard screens to W5. | W3 was the first org wave to touch the LIVE sync engine (sync.js and db.js). Keeping it client-only meant the change had to be safe against BOTH today's cloud and tomorrow's cloud, which is a much stronger test than building both halves together and letting them assume each other. | Rules out 'build the Logic App and the client together in one wave'. Rules out any client change that only works once the cloud side lands — every W3 behaviour must degrade to today's behaviour when the cloud says nothing new. | Claude, converged with Codex + AGY 2026-07-12 | `AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-W3-SCOPE.md:16-19, :101-103; build commit 7aa4733` |
| W3-1 | When a store leaves a device's list, the device must first push everything it has not yet uploaded, and only then delete the local copy. If the push fails (no internet), it deletes NOTHING and tries again next cycle. | The old code deleted first and pushed afterwards, so a staff member's offline entries for a store that changed hands were destroyed before they were ever uploaded. This was ground-truthed as a real, live data-loss bug, not a theoretical one. | Rules out 'purge now, push later'. Rules out accepting the loss as acceptable collateral of an ownership change. Rules out a partial purge that drops some rows and keeps others. | Claude, from Codex + AGY findings 2026-07-12 | `AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-W3-SCOPE.md:63-79 (design W3-1); gap mapped at sync.js:1889 / db.js:381` |
| W3-SR-2 | The local clear-out runs as ONE single all-or-nothing database transaction — read, check, and rewrite happen inside one locked block. | The first attempted fix was proven wrong on re-audit: the app reads and rewrites in two separate database operations, so a write from a second browser tab landing in the gap was wiped out completely, and the safety re-check afterwards saw nothing wrong because the row was already gone. Only one atomic block excludes that window. | Rules out the 'refresh then persist' pattern and any check-then-act fix. Rules out a post-write re-check as a substitute for a lock — it cannot see a row that has already been deleted. | Claude, from converged AGY + Codex re-audit 2026-07-12 | `AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-W3-SCOPE.md:45 (W3-SR-2, amended at R2)` |

Rules out 'we checked at the entry point' as an argument — the entry point was proven not to be the entry point. | Claude, from Codex R5-2 2026-07-12 | `AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-W3-SCOPE.md:59 (W3-SR-16), :76-79` |

| W4.2-P1 | A line's discount is looked up in three steps, most specific first: a rate set for that exact product at that store, then a global rate for that product, then the store's general rate. Dated ranges mean past lines keep resolving their own dates' rates and retroactive rewriting dies. | Rules out storing a discount as a single current number. Rules out 'just remember to not change the rate'. Once any dated pricing has been served, anything broken or missing shows a loud PRICING DATA ERROR at 0% — never a quietly-plausible old number. A pricing error at dispatch REJECTS the dispatch. | A silent fallback is the most dangerous failure mode here: it produces a believable invoice that is wrong, and nobody ever finds out. Refusing loudly forces a Director to fix the rate before stock moves, which is the only point where it is cheap to fix. | Rules out 'use the last known good rate'. Rules out storing a zero or a null as if it were a real rate. Rules out letting a supply dispatch through with unresolved pricing. | Claude, from Codex-2 / Codex R2-1 / Codex R3-4 2026-07-14 | `AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-W4.2-LENS-SCOPE.md:31-35` |

| RB-2 | The dangerous half of the rolled_back rule: a correction may be marked rolled back ONLY when the route can prove the change never landed AND can undo everything durable it already wrote. 'Publish may have landed' means ATTEMPTED, not failed. One case (S-WALK-3b) is deliberately NOT parked: it is the A/B anchor proving a future fix targets the real problem rather than taking a shortcut. | Rules out deleting the six mutations; rules out reading a green mutation run as a clean bill of health for C2; rules out un-parking S-WALK-3b. | Claude, under Kunal's parking decision 2026-07-29 | `audit-artifacts/check-gates-mutation.js:314-341; commit 726febe` |

| C2-COORD-5STATE | Only one job may touch the books at a time, governed by a single shared five-state 'who has the lock' record. Every state has a defined way to take over from a crashed holder, and recovery TAKES OWNERSHIP and drops a marker so a stalled worker that wakes up later can never finish its half-done job. | A stalled worker could otherwise wake up after recovery had already cleaned up after it and publish a change pointing at a record that no longer exists. The design also fixed the opposite failure: an unconditional 'release the lock' could let a stalled worker clobber a live successor's lock. And no state may strand the machine on a crashed holder — every one has a takeover rule. | Rules out unconditional lock releases anywhere (including the archive job's); rules out recovery acting on a worker that is still alive; rules out any state with no takeover path. | auditor-driven (AGY F1/F1b, Codex 4a/4b), folded by Claude 2026-07-24 (R1/R2 folds), converged 2026-07-25 | `AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-W44-C2-DESIGN.md:611-667` |
| C2-OUTCOME-BY-READ | After any attempt to publish a change, the route decides what actually happened by RE-READING the data — never by trusting the success/failure reply it got back. It only undoes work after positively reading proof the change did not land. | The classic lost-acknowledgement problem: the change applies, the reply gets lost, and a naive route then rolls back a correction that had genuinely gone through. If the answer is ambiguous or unreadable, the job stays open for recovery rather than guessing. | Rules out deciding rollback on an HTTP error; rules out any silent rollback of a possibly-published correction. | auditor-driven (Codex 5), folded by Claude 2026-07-24 | `AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-W44-C2-DESIGN.md:45,658` |
| C2-RESERVE-LAST | The 'sign this record out for work' step was moved DOWN to the last possible moment before the point of no return — and the fix was that move, not adding clean-up deletes. Its position is now pinned by an automated rule, not by memory. | Arming the route armed a trap. The sign-out book refuses duplicates and the route has no delete anywhere, and there were five refusal lanes BELOW the sign-out step. So a single refused correction would have made its target permanently uncorrectable by anyone, forever. | Rules out solving it with compensating deletes at each refusal lane; rules out anyone moving the step back up on the basis that 'it reads better there' — a mechanical reachability rule now blocks that. | Claude (raised against the 46-edit wave before it shipped) 2026-07-28 | `HANDOVER.md:483-486; PASTE-TO-AUDITORS-C2-LA.md:24-30` |

The narrowing is scoped to that one seal format on purpose; the equivalent format used by the archive run records deliberately keeps the strict distinction. | Rules out applying the collapse to the other seal formats; rules out treating this as a quiet fold — it changes a rule both auditors had already passed. | Claude, flagged to auditors as QZ1 2026-07-28 | `HANDOVER.md:487-489; PASTE-TO-AUDITORS-C2-LA.md:31-36` |

| H9 | If the system ever detects that its core safety rule has been broken, archives and exports stop for the WHOLE business, not just one store, until a director resolves it. | H10 | A deletion made on a store device now reaches OTHER devices only after the server has accepted it — in practice one sync round-trip later, seconds to minutes. | F-25 | 'No store device — including franchisee stores — receives the app until ALL the cloud work is done and audited.' The absolute rollout gate. | Set when the plan was to ship everything in one go, so that no shop would ever run a half-secured version. Treating it as a permanent refusal destroys a real stock count as a side effect of an admin action. Treating it as retryable leaves the counts sitting pending forever if no new PIN is ever set.
