## <a id="gaps"></a>Known gaps and surprises

Things that are not what a reasonable person would assume. These are the entries most
worth reading before planning any work.

### Logging in, roles and permissions

- The whole server-side login story is BUILT, TESTED AND DEPLOYED — and completely switched off. Not one person has ever logged in through the cloud. Two independent reasons: (1) the address of the login Logic App was never published to devices (AZURE-CHUNK9-WAVE-REVIEW.md line 44 calls it DORMANT ON PURPOSE), and (2) the settings channel that would deliver that address has never worked at all (sync.js:64 ships '%%CONFIG_URL%%'). Either one alone would be enough.
- The permission list a manager would think of as 'the security' is browser-only on the live site. It stops honest mistakes and catches regressions; it does not stop anyone who opens developer tools. This is written down and accepted for the internal alpha (CLAUDE.md §6) but is worth saying out loud before any wider rollout.
- Publishing the Account Access policy for the first time is not a no-op — it silently switches ON a real workflow change: a basic store account can no longer receive a Head Office delivery without keying in the 24-hour PIN (index.html:1054). It is Kunal's own 7 July decision, the screen warns about it, but it will surprise staff on the day.
- The 10-minute screen lock has no escape hatch. There is no 'sign out' or 'not me' on the lock screen, and on the working branch an account created through the cloud path gets no local PIN at all (index.html:3714) — so that person would be trapped behind an un-dismissable overlay with 'No PIN set. Contact admin.' and would have to close the browser.
- On the working branch, backups now strip password and PIN scrambles (index.html:3885). Restore that backup and nobody can sign in and nobody has a screen-lock PIN. The live site does NOT do this (main index.html:3295 has the shorter deny list), so restore works fine today — but this is a live trip-wire waiting on the cutover.
- The code comment at index.html:3886 still says password and PIN hashes are 'intentionally kept' in backups. The code on the same object strips them. The comment is stale — the code wins.
- There is no failed-login limit in the browser at all. Unlimited password guesses, unlimited screen-PIN guesses. The 5-strikes-then-lock behaviour exists only in the cloud Logic App, which is dormant.
- 'Territory manager' is a fully wired role — it has menu items, permissions, a badge colour and a dropdown entry — and there has never been a Territory manager account. Worse, Stock Overview (index.html:2778) does not treat them as store-scoped, so a Territory manager would see ALL stores regardless of which stores they were assigned.
- Auth.isAtLeast() (index.html:943) ranks franchisee BELOW store manager, which is backwards for this business. Nothing uses it and the comment right beside it warns not to, but it is sitting there looking usable. Auth.canSeeStore() (index.html:949) is written and never called by anything.
- One permission gate escaped the central list: the transfer hub's 'New Transfer' button still checks a hard-coded role array (phase2.js:1208), so a Director changing the access policy would not change who sees that button.

### Logging stock movements

- THE APP'S OWN STOCK-DRIFT DETECTOR IS SWITCHED OFF. Stock._verifyCacheIntegrity (index.html:1465) re-counts everything the slow way and compares it against the fast running totals — exactly the check you would want running to catch stock numbers going wrong. It is built, it is proven by two sentinels (S-208, S-213), and grep confirms the ONLY callers in the entire codebase are those two tests. Nothing in the app ever runs it. Its own comment says 'call from console'.
- A TRANSFER LOGGED THROUGH THIS WIZARD ONLY MOVES ONE END. 'Stock Out → Store Transfer → Karrinyup' takes the units off the sending store and does nothing at Karrinyup. The receiving store must log their own Stock In or the units disappear from the company total. _submitLog (index.html:2176) creates exactly one line, and nothing writes a counterpart. This is by design — the proper two-sided flow is the separate Transfers screen — but there is no warning on the screen telling staff the other end still has to receive it. This is the easiest way for staff to make the numbers wrong.
- 'DAMAGED — WRITE OFF' RETURNS STILL ADD STOCK. The Return Condition dropdown (index.html:1992-2001) is recorded and displayed, and that is all it does — grep-verified, returnCondition appears nowhere in the stock maths or in any report. A return marked 'Damaged — Write Off' puts a sellable-looking unit back on the shelf count and never reaches the wastage report. Someone has to remember to log a second movement to Wastage/Damage.
- THE HEAD-OFFICE-TO-FRANCHISE PRICE STAMP CAN NEVER FIRE FROM THIS SCREEN. index.html:2198-2207 freezes the sell price and discount on a directly-logged Head Office supply, and refuses the log if pricing is stale. But it requires the movement's source-store-id to be exactly 'head_office', and the wizard only sets a source-store-id for 'Another Store' / 'Franchise Store' — and the store list offered there deliberately excludes the Head Office warehouse (index.html:2076). So a franchise logging 'Stock In from HO Warehouse' gets NO price stamp, yet the movement STILL lands on their invoice through the text fallback at index.html:4876. Built, unreachable from the UI, and no sentinel covers this path.
- THE 'FRANCHISE TRANSFER PRICING' BANNER SHOWS ON THE WRONG MOVEMENTS. index.html:2008 names its variable destStore but assigns it the location selected at the TOP of the page, not the destination. So if Head Office selects 'Cockburn' and logs an ordinary Customer Sale, the purple 'Franchise Transfer Pricing' box appears with a sell price and discount. It also reads the flat franchiseDiscount scalar, not the dated era-aware Pricing lens, so the number it shows can disagree with what the invoice would actually bill.
- A STALE COMMENT IN THE CODE CONTRADICTS THE CODE. index.html:2191-2193 says the frozen sale price is 'local-only for now — the snapshot is NOT pushed to SharePoint yet'. It IS pushed (sync.js:1309) and the staging cloud has the column (AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-STAGING-LEDGER.md:25). An engineer reading only the comment would rebuild something that already exists.
- ANYONE CAN LOG A MOVEMENT AND SIGN IT WITH ANY NAME. There is no permission check on the Log Movement page — the route (index.html:6178) has no gate, and _submitLog has none. The 'Your Name' field is free text, unconnected to the logged-in account. The one place the app does record the verified account is on deletions and undos (index.html:2286, 2340). This was already ground-truthed once in audit-artifacts/AA-STAGING-APPLY-PLAN.md (line 576 — local working analysis, not in git).
- 'FRANCHISE OFFICE' AS A STOCK-IN SOURCE NEVER ASKS WHICH ONE. It is offered in the source dropdown (index.html:1805) but is not in STORE_PICKER_FROM (index.html:1836), so no follow-up store picker appears and the counterparty is left blank. Ran the option lists today to confirm. There is also a dead reason set for 'Franchise Store' under Stock IN (index.html:1826) that the UI can never reach.
- THE SEED NAMES WHITFORD INCORRECTLY. It is seeded as 'Whitford City' (id whitford) rather than the real 'Whitfords'. (The two closed salons that used to appear here are gone: Ardross and Bunbury were DELETED from the seed on 2026-08-02 (33 practice movements, 12 threshold entries and both store rows removed); test/check-seed-integrity.js now fails if either reappears.)

### Stock takes

- The stock-take email to Logistics has NEVER been able to send, and never says so. The app looks up the email address from the cloud config channel, and that channel has never worked (sync.js:64 ships the literal placeholder '%%CONFIG_URL%%'). With no address, index.html:2559 posts to 'about:blank' and swallows the failure — the user still gets a green 'Stock take complete' toast. Identical on the live branch. Anyone expecting a stock-take email in the logistics inbox will never get one and will get no warning.
- Directors have no menu link to Stock Take. Their sidebar (index.html:6051-6078) and phone bottom bar (index.html:6145-6152) have no 'Stock Take' entry — yet the Pending Approvals panel only exists on that page (index.html:2419). The only route in is the 'Stock Take' quick-action button on the Dashboard (index.html:4504). There is also no badge or notification anywhere telling a director that counts are waiting for sign-off, so a submitted count can sit pending indefinitely with nobody knowing.
- Today's stock-take PIN only exists on the device it was set on. It is stored as a hash in that device's own local storage (db.js:92, 135, 270, 288) and syncs nowhere. So a director setting the PIN on their phone does not unlock Stock Take on the Booragoon computer — someone with director access has to set it physically on each store device. The cloud version that fixes this (one PIN, all devices, secret kept server-side) is fully built and tested but switched off.
- The Head Office warehouse cannot be stock-taken. The store dropdown filters out anything of type 'warehouse' unless it is flagged as a franchise office (index.html:2410-2412), and head_office is a plain warehouse in the seed (index.html:610). The Cockburn Franchise Office slips through only because it carries isFranchiseOffice. So the single biggest pile of stock in the business has no counting screen.
- Stock Reconciliation will baseline off a REJECTED or still-PENDING count. index.html:4797 picks the most recent stock take for a store with no check on status. Neither of those was ever applied to stock, so the 'expected' figure is wrong and the report would light up with phantom discrepancies for that store.
- Discrepancy History values losses at the RETAIL selling price, not at cost (index.html:2644). The 'Value impact' headline therefore reads as lost sales revenue, which is roughly double the real cost of the stock. Worth deciding which number Kunal actually wants before staff are trained on it.
- There is no stock take history screen. A clean count (everything matched) is saved and then effectively vanishes — Discrepancy History only shows approved counts that HAD differences, and Reconciliation only shows the single latest count per store. Nobody can answer 'when did Claremont last do a full count and who did it?' from the app.
- A rejected count is a dead end. There is no way for the counter to correct it and resubmit — index.html:2592 permanently refuses anything that is not still pending, so the shop has to start the whole count from scratch.
- Deleting a product tries to clean it out of past stock takes and silently does nothing. index.html:3520 filters on s.lines, but stock takes store their rows under 'items', not 'lines' — so the condition is always false and every stock take is kept untouched. Keeping the history is arguably the right outcome, but the code does not do what it was written to do, and nobody noticed.
- The live site's stock-take IDs are just 'st_' plus the millisecond (main index.html:2081/2145). The random-suffix hardening exists only on the working branch. Low risk while counts never leave the device, but it is a real live-vs-branch difference.
- The link between an approved count and the adjustments it created (the stockTakeId field, index.html:2603) is not part of the cloud sync contract (sync.js:1287-1320, 1366-1420). If stock takes ever do sync, the double-apply guard at index.html:2594 will not see adjustments that arrived from another device.
- AA-20 IS SETTLED AND THE CODE PROVES IT. Clearing the PIN was built as an instant kill-switch across all devices, not wait-for-expiry. RESOLVED: the deployed code implements instant revocation — grants carry a pinEpoch and clearing or changing the PIN bumps it, so every outstanding grant dies immediately (azure-functions/src/functions/accessPolicy.js:103-117; the app drops its local grant at sync.js:441-446). Decided by Kunal 2026-07-10 and recorded in AZURE-CHUNK-AA-LA-CHANGES.md. Several project documents still list it under decisions awaiting the owner — they are stale.
- There is no completeness check on a count at all. Partial counting is the only mode — you type numbers for whatever you want and submit. Nothing warns that 300 products were skipped, and nothing records that the take was partial.

### Transfers between stores

- Nothing in the app can create a DRAFT transfer. The New Transfer screen always sends immediately; the only code that asks for a draft is createFromStockTake, which nothing calls. So the Drafts tab is permanently empty, and the whole Draft screen (confirm each line, adjust quantities, then Submit & Send) is unreachable — even though it is fully built, audited and partly sentinel-covered.
- The 'auto-build a restock transfer from a stock take' function (phase2.js:360) is dead code with zero callers anywhere in the repo. It was already identified as dead in BLIND-AUDIT-TRACKING.md:401 and never removed or wired up.
- No transfer email has ever been sendable. Both the 'transfer sent' and 'transfer completed' emails depend on an address delivered through the cloud config channel, and that channel has never worked (sync.js:64 ships the placeholder '%%CONFIG_URL%%'). The code logs '[Transfer] email URL not configured' and silently returns (phase2.js:234). The Azure email Logic App itself exists and is fine.
- Transit Void surprises people every time: stock leaves the sending store on SUBMIT, not on receipt. While goods are on the road they exist on nobody's shelf, so the company-wide total dips. Only a Director cancelling can put them back without a receipt.
- The franchise submit gate would block EVERY Head Office to Cockburn transfer on the day the working branch goes live. It requires a confirmed, fresh pricing observation from the server, and the config channel that would supply one has never worked. The repo's own publish-safety check already calls this out as a cutover blocker (test/check-publish-safety.js:171).
- Dated/era-aware franchise pricing on transfers is BUILT and PROVEN, not missing — each supply line freezes the sell price and discount of the day it was sent, and older in-transit transfers are priced using the day they were SENT rather than the day they arrived. It is switched off purely because nothing yet serves a pricing configuration.
- Everything that makes transfers work BETWEEN devices — the receiving store seeing an incoming transfer created on a Head Office laptop, the double-receive conflict screen, the 'stock hasn't landed yet' warnings — is built, audited and proven on the staging cloud, but is switched OFF twice over: it is on the working branch (not the live site), and its two cloud addresses come through the config channel that has never worked.
- Completed transfers self-delete after 30 days. Worse, the automatic version looks broken: it removes them from memory then calls DB.commit(), which deliberately does NOT rewrite the transfers table (db.js:111-131), so they can come back after a reload. The manual pruneCompleted() has an explicit fix for exactly this (phase2.js:787) that the automatic path never received. No test covers either.
- If a draft ever were created, the Draft screen would open every quantity box at 0 and show 'Shortfall: 0', because it reads i.qty / i.shortfall while saved lines store the number under sentQty (phase2.js:1427 and 1452 vs phase2.js:326-330). Submitting without retyping every number would then be refused by the whole-number rule.
- The 'Needs Receiving' tab is empty for a Director or Head Office account, because it means 'in transit to a store I am attached to' and those accounts normally have no stores attached. Correct by design, but it reads like a bug.
- The newer transfer statuses (conflict, stock_pending, stock_mismatch) have no badge colour defined in the transfer stylesheet (phase2.js:907-914), so those rows would render with an unstyled tag in the hub.
- The seed store list is now 9 stores and matches the business. It still names Whitford as 'Whitford City' rather than 'Whitfords'. Both the Cockburn store and the Cockburn Franchise Office are flagged as franchise, so Head Office supply to either one is gated. Ardross and Bunbury were DELETED from the seed on 2026-08-02 (33 practice movements, 12 threshold entries and both store rows removed); test/check-seed-integrity.js now fails if either reappears.
- Transfer.list() — the module's own filtered list function — is not used by the app at all; only the test harness calls it. The Transfers Hub does its own separate filtering, so the two could drift.

### Reports, analytics and exports

- THE SAME WORDS MEAN DIFFERENT MONEY. 'Total Stock Value' on the Reports Overview is valued at COST (index.html:3284 uses Stock.currentCost). The identically-labelled tile on the Dashboard and the 'Total Portfolio' tile on Stock Overview are valued at RETAIL (Stock.totalValue, index.html:1568-1576). Two screens will show two very different numbers under the same heading.
- THE LIVE FRANCHISE INVOICE STILL REWRITES HISTORY. On the live site (branch main, index.html:4120-4145) every invoice line is priced from today's catalogue price and today's discount percentage. Change Cockburn's discount and every invoice you have ever produced silently changes. The dated fix IS built and proven on the working branch, but it is not deployed and nothing serves it a pricing_config, so it never activates.
- THE DISCOUNT MODEL KUNAL DECIDED DOES NOT EXIST. Cockburn is seeded as a single flat 25% (index.html:604). Kunal's model is a store rate, overridden by a PRODUCT TYPE rate, overridden by a per-product rate - Retail 50%, professional ~25%. There is no by-type tier anywhere in client or server code. Today's invoice would under-discount every retail item by half.
- STOCK SHIPPED TO THE COCKBURN SHOP IS NEVER BILLED. The invoice only bills stores flagged as a franchise OFFICE (index.html:4886). The Cockburn shop itself carries a franchiseDiscount of 25 in the seed (index.html:603) but is deliberately excluded, and sentinel S-106 pins that as correct. So if Head Office ever ships direct to the shop instead of the office, that stock appears on no invoice at all.
- THE 'ALL TRANSACTIONS CSV' IGNORES TWO OF THE FILTERS ON SCREEN. Pages._exportCSV (index.html:3381-3391) applies only the store and date filters - the category and product-type filters are dropped, so the spreadsheet can contain rows the screen was hiding. It also includes deleted tombstone rows, which the other movements CSV correctly excludes.
- REPORTS TRUNCATE SILENTLY. The Transaction Log and All Movements show only the newest 500 rows; Dead Stock, Sell-Through, Reconciliation and Discrepancy History cap at 200. None of them says so on screen.
- STORE COMPARISON 'VALUE' IS NOT FROZEN. The A vs B value charts and columns (index.html:3102-3103, 3195) multiply units by the CURRENT catalogue price, so a price rise today makes last year look richer. The Director-only Gross Sales column right next to it does it properly using the frozen price. Two columns on one table, two different rules.
- WHITFORD IS NAMED WRONG IN EVERY REPORT. It is seeded as 'Whitford City' rather than the real 'Whitfords', so that is the label shown in every store dropdown, comparison chip and stock grid. (The two closed salons that used to appear alongside it are gone: Ardross and Bunbury were DELETED from the seed on 2026-08-02 (33 practice movements, 12 threshold entries and both store rows removed); test/check-seed-integrity.js now fails if either reappears.)
- THE MARGIN REPORT IS HIDDEN IN SETTINGS. It is a tab inside Settings (index.html:5043) rather than under Analytics with the other reports, so a Director looking for 'how much do we make' in the reports menu will not find it.
- 'COPY SUMMARY' ON THE ALERTS PAGE IGNORES THE STORE FILTER. index.html:2737 calls Stock.alerts() with no arguments, so it always copies every store even when the screen is filtered to one.
- THE REPORTS PAGE ITSELF HAS NO PERMISSION CHECK. Store Comparison (index.html:2945) and Cost Management (index.html:5178) both guard the top of the page; hoReports does not. The only thing keeping staff out is the absence of a menu link.
- DEAD STOCK COUNTS A TRANSFER-OUT AS MOVEMENT. index.html:4666 marks any OUT as activity, so shipping slow stock to another store makes it look like it is selling.

### Products, categories and stock levels

- Minimum and optimum stock levels NEVER SYNC. The word "thresholds" does not appear anywhere in sync.js — they live only in the browser storage of the device you typed them on. So every low-stock badge, every alert and the whole Reorder List are driven by numbers that exist on one phone or PC. Set them at head office and Karrinyup's phone still sees nothing. This is arguably the single biggest gap in this area.
- "Remove" on a product never reaches the cloud. Removing a CATEGORY or a PRODUCT TYPE flags itself for publishing; removing a PRODUCT does not (index.html:3516-3522 has no Sync._markCatalogueDirty call, unlike :3571 and :3576). Even after a director presses Publish, other devices would keep showing the product forever.
- Changing a product's franchise discount also never reaches the cloud on the current (pre-server) path — index.html:3442 saves it locally and stops. Same for supplier name / contact / lead days (index.html:4326-4333). Those details only travel by accident, if someone later edits the same product's name or price, which flags the whole row.
- "Remove Product" is more destructive than the wording suggests. As well as hiding the product it permanently deletes that product's purchase-cost history and edits it out of past stock-take records (index.html:3520). The confirm box only says "This will deactivate the product."
- Removed categories and product types stay visibly on the Categories & Types screen, with working Edit and Remove buttons, looking exactly like live ones. They only disappear from the dropdowns where you'd pick them. A manager who removes a category will reasonably think it didn't work.
- 141 of the 192 seeded products have no sell price, so the Products screen is a wall of amber "⚠ Missing" warnings. (Only 51 are priced.) None of the 192 has Retail/Consumable set either, so everything currently behaves as Retail — including wax and disposables, which will therefore appear in sales and sell-through reports until someone marks them.
- The franchise discount model Kunal decided today — universal store rate, then a PRODUCT TYPE rate, then a per-product rate — has no product-type tier anywhere in the code. The built chain (index.html:1234-1237) is: this store's rate for this product → company-wide rate for this product → this store's default rate. That middle tier has to be added.
- The Lead Days column on the Thresholds screen does not exist on the live site — it is only on the unreleased working branch. Today the only place to set lead days is the Suppliers screen, which is per product, not per store. The Reorder List already prefers the product-level value over the store-level one.
- There are two different screens editing the same minimum/optimum numbers with different rules: Settings > Thresholds (per store, one product at a time, with a Clear button) and the Optimum Levels screen (bulk, with a Global "all stores" option). They are easy to confuse and one of them can quietly overwrite the other's work.
- Every product record carries weight, freight, customs and tax fields that nothing ever reads or edits. They look like real product attributes in a backup file and are not.
- A product with no minimum set anywhere is invisible to the alerts page and the Reorder List — it can run to zero with nobody being warned. Only the Stock Overview screen says anything, with a grey "No threshold" badge.
- The device refuses a price over $1,000,000; the cloud allows up to $10,000,000. Not a bug (the device is stricter) but the two rules are written in two places and have already drifted apart.

### Costs and pricing

- There is NO cost data anywhere. All 192 seeded products have costPrice: null (index.html:408-633) and the seeded cost history is an empty list (index.html:641). So today the Cost Management screen would say '0/192 products with cost data' and '192 products missing cost', and every margin, wastage-at-cost, on-hand-at-cost and Total-Cost figure reads $0 or 'No cost'. Nothing is broken — nobody has entered a cost yet.
- There is NO screen anywhere to set a store's franchise discount. Cockburn's 25% is hard-coded seed data (index.html:603-604). 'Add Store' always writes null (index.html:3550) and 'Edit Store' has no discount field at all (index.html:3551). A new franchise store would be billed at 0% (loudly flagged 'NOT SET') until someone sets a per-product discount or edits the seed. This is the single biggest gap in the area.
- The by-PRODUCT-TYPE discount tier Kunal decided on today (Cockburn: retail 50%, professional ~25%) does not exist in any form. The code has exactly two tiers: a store/office-wide rate and a per-product override (index.html:3418, :4976). There is no way to say 'all retail products get 50%'.
- The dated COST history never leaves the device. Deliveries do sync between devices (records.js:604-608), but the fold recreates the delivery record only — it never recreates the cost-history rows, and nothing pushes or pulls the costHistory table. Only the single current cost can travel, and only via the gated corporate-costs blob. Two devices could end up with different cost histories for the same product.
- Two screens both say 'Total Stock Value' and mean different things. Dashboard / Stock Overview / Settings>Stores value stock at the SELL price (Stock.totalValue, index.html:1568-1575). Reports > Overview values it at COST (index.html:3284). Nothing on either screen says which.
- The 'higher wins' rule means a cheaper delivery NEVER lowers a product's cost (index.html:5575-5577). A supplier price drop will not show up in margins until someone types a manual cost entry. That is intentional in the code but has no test and may not be what Kunal wants.
- Products carry freight, customs and tax fields written at creation time (index.html:3491) that nothing anywhere ever reads. Dead leftovers from an older design. Freight/tax live on the DELIVERY, not the product.
- There is no 'Customs' input on the delivery form any more — customs is meant to go inside 'Tax & Duty' (index.html:5350). The screens still display a customs figure if an old record has one (index.html:5617, :5668), so the word appears in the UI with no way to enter it.
- A product-level franchise discount of 0% cannot be expressed. The dropdown has no 0% option and a stored 0 is treated as 'not set, inherit the store rate' (index.html:3441). 100% means free.
- The whole account-level cost-visibility layer — 'see cost prices', the cost-strip on publish, the gated corporate-costs fetch, the scrub-on-revoke — is NOT on the live site. main has no seeCost, no corpCostsUrl and no catalogue-publish path. The live site runs the older, simpler client-only cost handling.
- The server's delivery money validator (azure-functions/src/functions/validateMoney.js) is genuinely deployed and wired into the staging ingest Logic App, but has ZERO automated tests — even though its own closing comment says it is 'exported for unit/sentinel tests'.
- The dated franchise pricing lens is fully built and heavily proven (client Pricing module, index.html:1162-1252; server topology.js; test/topology-proof.js re-run clean at 256 PASS today) and has never once run. No pricing_config has ever been written, no pricing Logic App exists on staging (zero 'pricing' mentions in AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-STAGING-LEDGER.md), and the config channel itself is dead at sync.js:64/:691.

### Managing users and stores

- Editing the Cockburn Franchise Office and pressing Save turns off its franchise flag. index.html:3552 recalculates "is a franchise" purely from the type dropdown, and the office's type is Warehouse — so a Save with no changes at all silently drops it out of Franchise Invoicing. This is live on main today.
- There is no way whatsoever to create a franchise office. The isFranchiseOffice flag is set in exactly one place in the entire codebase — the starting data at index.html:604. Neither the Add Store form, the Edit Store form, nor the cloud validation (catalogueMerge.js:59-64) knows the field exists. A new franchisee cannot be onboarded from the app.
- There is no editor for a store's franchise discount — confirmed by reading every write to franchiseDiscount. Cockburn's 25% is a number typed into the starting data. The cloud would accept a new value (catalogueMerge.js:62) and Franchise Invoicing reads it, but nothing in the app can send one.
- The product-type tier of Kunal's discount model does not exist anywhere. The code supports a shop rate and a per-product rate only. Cockburn's real rates (Retail 50%, professional ~25%) cannot be expressed.
- Leaving the PIN box blank when adding a user silently sets that person's PIN to 1234 (index.html:3701). No warning, no note, and the 4-digit PIN is what unlocks the idle screen lock.
- The Edit Store form has only two fields — name and type. That is the complete set of things a Director can change about a location today.
- The lock screen has no escape hatch. No Logout, no Cancel — only the correct 4-digit PIN. And accounts created through the cloud path get no local PIN at all (index.html:3715), so every one of them would be permanently stuck at that screen on their first idle lock.
- Backups strip password and PIN scrambles on the way out AND on the way in. Restoring a backup onto a brand-new device gives you the whole user list with nobody able to log in. The code comment at index.html:3893 still claims the hashes are kept — the comment is stale, the code strips them.
- The whole Account Access permission grid — a Director-only screen with 20 capabilities × 6 roles, per-person exceptions and a password-re-prompt map — is fully built, has 67 passing server proofs plus ~20 sentinels, is partly deployed to the staging cloud, and has never been seen by anyone. It renders "⚠ Access policy NOT ACTIVE" and Publish returns "endpoint not configured".
- The "Confirm your password" re-prompt is wired into nine privileged actions and is currently a no-op — index.html:4236 detects that cloud accounts are off and returns success without showing the box.
- The store/franchise change wizard (open a shop, onboard a franchisee, convert a company store to a franchise, buy one back) exists as ~670 lines of proven server logic with 256 passing tests and absolutely no screen in front of it. The two real cases are due Sep-Oct 2026.
- Store types are mostly decoration. Of the five options, only Warehouse and Franchise change any behaviour — Inline, Kiosk and Online are labels.
- A store can never be deleted, only deactivated. And once cloud delivery of the shop list works, removing a shop from the cloud will not remove it from a device — the merge only adds and updates (sync.js:882-901).
- The device-side Add User has no minimum password length and no username character rules. Those checks exist only on the cloud path, which does not run.
- Adding or editing a user only affects the device you are standing at. Users are not part of catalogue publishing, so a person added on the Karrinyup computer never reaches the Whitford computer.
- Head Office cannot manage users at all — no menu link and the page bounces them — but Head Office CAN add, edit and deactivate stores.
- Promoting someone to Director or Head Office hides the store tick-boxes but does not clear ticks already made, so the old store list is quietly saved onto the account.

### Sync, offline and backup

- THE BIGGEST ONE, restated with the mechanism: sync has never started on ANY device, ever — not just the config extras. Sync.init() at sync.js:2595 tries the config address, gets the un-substituted %%CONFIG_URL%% placeholder, bails at sync.js:691, then tries the session cache which is empty, and RETURNS at sync.js:2603 showing 'Cloud sync unavailable — config not found. Contact Kunal.' Nothing after that line runs. So there is no 30-second poll, no leader election, no cross-tab refresh, and no send-on-reconnect on the live site. The whole sync layer is inert, not merely under-configured.
- The deploy workflow was never capable of filling the placeholder in. .github/workflows/deploy-swa.yml is 18 lines: checkout, then upload with skip_app_build: true. There is no build step, no substitution step, and no secret named CONFIG_URL. The belief that 'CI replaces it at deploy' is written in three places (BLIND-AUDIT-TRACKING.md:86, :92, :101) and was used to DISMISS this exact finding across all six blind auditors. The documents are wrong and the code is right.
- A test actively enforces the broken state. test/verify-app.js:111 asserts "Sync.CONFIG_URL is the deploy placeholder" with the comment 'raw repo should keep %%CONFIG_URL%% so it boots offline'. A green gate is currently certifying the thing that stops sync from ever working.
- The live app CAN be made to sync by hand, but only for one browser session. Settings → Cloud Sync lets a Director paste the push and pull addresses in directly (index.html:4159-4175). Sync.saveConfig writes them to sessionStorage (sync.js:983), which the browser wipes when the tab or browser closes. So it works until the tablet is closed, then silently stops. Nothing on screen warns that it is temporary.
- Nothing in the app ever tells a user how much work is waiting to be sent. There is no 'N changes pending' badge and no list of rows the server rejected. The push code comments say rejected rows are 'surfaced to an admin' — grep for _rejected across index.html, phase2.js and records.js finds only one hit, in records.js, unrelated to a screen. If sync were switched on and started rejecting rows, the only evidence would be a passing toast and the diagnostics file.
- The backup file downloaded from the LIVE site contains staff password AND PIN hashes. main's _REUSABLE_AUTH_KEYS (git show main:index.html:3295) has no 'password', 'pin' or 'proof' entry, and the live export is not password-confirmed at all. The working branch fixes both (index.html:3885, index.html:3908). Given the data/bob-stock-data.json incident found today, this is the same class of exposure in a file a Director is encouraged to download.
- 'Undo Last Restore' works by a side effect, not by design. It writes the snapshot into the OLD localStorage key and reloads (index.html:4055), relying on the one-time IndexedDB upgrade routine (db.js:1298) to pick it up and overwrite everything. The restore itself does the same (index.html:4014). It is correct today, but if anyone ever deletes that upgrade routine as 'legacy', both restore and undo break silently.
- The 'Stock last synced' label is deliberately not called 'Last synced' — because on the live design transfers, deliveries and stock takes were never going to sync between devices at all, only stock levels and the catalogue. The record-steps sync that fixes this exists and is heavily tested (S-171..S-185) but is server-phase only.
- Background sync and the install banner do nothing on iPhone/iPad. The Background Sync API is Chrome-family only, and Safari has no install prompt — so the 'Install BOB Stock' banner will never appear on an iPad, and staff would have to know to use Share → Add to Home Screen. There is no iOS-specific instruction anywhere.
- sw.js contains a complete push-notification handler (sw.js:118-134) that can never fire — nothing subscribes, nothing requests permission, and there is no server that sends one. It is dead code that reads like a shipped feature.
- The service worker is network-first for everything. On a salon wifi that is joined but effectively dead, every file waits for the network to fail before the offline copy is used, so the app feels frozen rather than instantly offline — which is the opposite of what 'offline-first' suggests to a manager.
- The diagnostics file a Director sends to support is stamped 'bob-stock-v10' (index.html:6311) while the app is actually on v14. Anyone diagnosing from that file would be looking at the wrong version.
- The live site's sync code is an older, materially different generation. main's pull still pages with $skip (git show main:sync.js:923), which SharePoint throttles past roughly 5,000 rows — that would have broken the first onboarding of any new or long-offline device. The row-number cursor, the auth envelope, record-steps, scope isolation and the topology hold do not exist on main at all.

### The local database and data safety

- The live site is running an OLD database layer. I downloaded db.js from gray-island-05e673800.7.azurestaticapps.net today: it is byte-identical to branch 'main' (1064 lines) and contains NONE of the newer work. No transfer/delivery step diary, no franchise data clean-out, no out-of-scope write lock, no archive shrinking, no server-rejection parking, no access-policy storage. Roughly a third of everything in db.js today is built, audited and unreachable by any real user.
- 'Reset the database' (db.js:1251) has a comment saying it is called from Settings. It is called from nowhere. There is no button. To clear a device you have to clear browser data by hand.
- The stock-cache health check (index.html:1464) that would tell you whether the on-hand numbers have drifted has no caller either — it is console-only. There is no 'check my numbers are consistent' button for a manager.
- Saving reference data silently deletes apostrophes, quotes and angle brackets from names AND IDs (db.js:326-341). "Kunal's Store" becomes "Kunals Store" with no message. This is intentional safety hardening but it will look like a bug to staff.
- 'Restore from backup' does not write to the database directly. It writes the backup into the OLD legacy storage slot and reloads, and the one-time legacy-migration code then rebuilds the database from it. So a code path labelled 'legacy, one-time' is actually load-bearing for restore — and it is the one path in the whole file with no test coverage at all.
- A side effect of that: after any restore, a full second copy of the database is left permanently parked in simple browser storage (as bob_stock_v4_migrated). The disaster-recovery path will serve that copy if the real database ever fails to open, which is why the recovery warning is worded so strongly.
- The seed still gives Cockburn a single flat 25% franchise discount rather than the Retail 50% / everything-else 25% split Kunal decided on 2026-08-02. Any device set up fresh today starts from that wrong rate. (The store list itself is now correct: Ardross and Bunbury were DELETED from the seed on 2026-08-02 (33 practice movements, 12 threshold entries and both store rows removed); test/check-seed-integrity.js now fails if either reappears.)
- The archive-shrinking feature is off for two independent reasons, not one: the code is not on the live site, AND the cloud config channel that would deliver the opening-balance summary has never worked. Fixing either alone changes nothing.
- The whole fire-and-forget write family (DB.save, addTransaction, addTransfer, updateTransfer, addTransactions, atomicTransferWrite, removeTransaction) now has zero callers in the app — every real write goes through the await-able versions. About 200 lines of db.js are dead weight kept for compatibility, and the saboteur/mutation tooling still references DB.save.

### The cloud brain (Azure Functions)

- THE CLOUD IS FIVE DAYS BEHIND THE CODE. The Function App bob-stock-money-fn was last deployed 2026-07-23 (confirmed via az: lastModified 2026-07-23T01:20:27, state Running). Since then attestRows.js (2026-07-28), correctionCompute.js (2026-07-28) and snapshotCompute.js (2026-07-26) and validateUser.js (2026-07-25) all changed. So four routes in the cloud are older than the repo, and the repo's proof suites are testing code that is not what is running.
- THE MISSING 15th ROUTE IS correctionCompute. The repo registers 15 routes; the cloud has 14. The absentee is the correction/deletion brain — deliberately parked on 2026-07-29, not lost. It also carries a known blocking defect: the record IDs it creates contain a colon, which the settlement engine's ID rule rejects, so deploying it today would abort an entire store's buy-back export.
- NOT A SINGLE ROUTE IS REACHABLE BY A REAL USER. I checked every live Logic App (bob-stock-push-v2, bob-stock-pull-v2, bob-stock-config, and the legacy bob-stock-push / bob-stock-pull / bob-stock-pull-v2-paged) — none of them contain a single reference to bob-stock-money-fn. Every workflow that calls a Function route is a *-staging one. The whole cloud brain is deployed, largely proven, and switched off.
- FOUR DEPLOYED ROUTES HAVE NO CALLER AT ALL, not even a staging one: buybackExport, topologyPlan, topologyResolve and validatePin. There is no buy-back export workflow and no topology-change workflow in the resource group, and the PIN check has never been wired into the login workflow. They are live endpoints nothing talks to.
- THREE ROUTES HAVE ZERO AUTOMATED TESTS: validateKeys (device login), validateMoney (delivery costs) and catalogueMerge (the product and store list publisher). catalogueMerge is the most concerning — it decides what every device sees, it strips cost prices, and nothing tests it. validateKeys has only a one-off cloud run from 2026-07-22 behind it.
- A DOCUMENTED SECURITY CLAIM IS NOW FALSE. validateKeys.js:22 states 'There is NO hash-emitting route anywhere'. mintUserCredential (validateUser.js:157-166) returns the salt and scrambled password to its caller. It is safe in practice (key-locked, Logic-App-only) but the code and the comment disagree and the comment has not been updated.
- THE FRANCHISE DISCOUNT ONLY HAS TWO TIERS, NOT THREE. topology.js:149-173 resolves a whole-store rate ('*') overridden by a per-product rate. There is no PRODUCT TYPE tier, so Kunal's decided model — store rate, then product-type rate, then per-product rate — cannot be expressed today. The engine is built and proven; the middle tier simply does not exist.
- REAL PRODUCTION BUGS WERE CAUGHT ONLY BY RUNNING IT ON THE CLOUD, NOT BY TESTS. The 2026-07-23 archive run found the archive stores its timestamp as text while the source stores it as a number — every single row was rejected and the lock stuck. All the local proofs passed because they ran against a test list with the wrong column type. Same run found IdempotencyKey had NEVER been copied to the archive.
- STAGING STILL HOLDS A LIVE TEST DIRECTOR ACCOUNT. srvaudit_director sits in UserCredentials_Staging with a real password in a local gitignored file, plus six regenerated store device keys. The cutover checklist says delete the account and rotate the keys before go-live — this is an outstanding obligation, not a done item.
- THE SETTLEMENT ENGINE IS FROZEN WITH KNOWN MONEY-CRITICAL WORK STILL OWED. buybackExport passes 170/170 today, but three checks are deliberately deferred to a return audit: treating an invalid tamper seal as a hard stop, catching a sealed row that claims to predate the sealing era, and enforcing the control seal (a tampered control row currently settles at 3.75 instead of 375).

### Archiving and long-term data

- The 'Load archived data' button ignores the date range you picked. Verified today against the deployed Logic App: the app sends the from/to dates at the top level of the request, but the cloud reads them nested one level deeper, so it always returns the first 5,000 archived rows in row order. Nobody caught this because every test mocks the server (AZURE-CHUNK-AA-LA-CHANGES.md §6 flagged it 2026-07-30; sync.js:1443 vs audit-artifacts/bob-stock-archive-pull-staging-REDACTED-2026-07-31.json).
- The archived-movement read does not ask for the money columns. Its column list is TransactionId, StoreId, ProductId, TxnType, Qty, TxnDate, TxnTimestamp, StaffName, Reason, DeviceId, TransferId, TargetTransactionId, SourceId — no supply price, no discount, no catalogue version, no sale price, no from/to labels. But the app's reader (sync.js:1426-1435) is written to consume exactly those. So archived rows pulled back for a report arrive with their money meaning stripped. That directly undercuts the whole reason the archive-carry upgrade was built in July.
- The catch-up upgrade is finished, audited by two external auditors, proven on 20,048 rows, has a written cutover runbook — and has NEVER been installed. Verified today: the live 'main' branch still uses the old download method. The failure mode it fixes is specifically a brand-new device or a long-offline device once the movement list passes ~5,000 rows. Nothing bad happens until then, which is exactly why it keeps not getting done.
- There is no way to actually start an archive run. No button, no schedule — the Logic App has a manual HTTP trigger only, and the caller must supply the cutoff row number, the retention window and the version number by hand. The agreed rule (5,000 rows or six months) exists only as a sentence in a document.
- The buy-back settlement engine is deployed to the staging cloud and nothing can reach it. It went live as a side effect of an unrelated July deploy. There is no route feeding it and no screen calling it — the word 'buyback' does not appear anywhere in the app's own files.
- A real archive run against the real list would have failed outright before July. The archive list stored the timestamp as text while the source list stored it as a number, so every insert was rejected and the run-lock was left stuck. The original Chunk 8 proofs ran against a test list that happened to use text, so they never showed it (AZURE-CHUNK-ORG-STAGING-LEDGER.md:89-95).
- The archive copy never carried the duplicate-protection key across, from the day it was built until it was found in July. That would have broken the seals on archived receipt rows and the duplicate check that spans both lists.
- Two different things in the app are called a 'snapshot'. The Reports screen has a 'Stock Snapshot' tab (index.html:3205) which is just a current stock-on-hand table — nothing to do with archiving. Worth renaming one of them before staff training.
- The correction-aware archive maths (the most heavily commented code in the file, roughly 120 lines of reasoning) is switched off by construction and has never run in the deployed world.

### The access policy system

- The entire Account Access system is invisible to every user right now. Verified today, not inferred: `git show main:index.html | grep -c editAccessPolicy` returns 0 and `git show main:sync.js | grep -c accessPolicy` returns 0. The live site serves 'main'. There is no Account Access tab, no policy, no PIN-to-receive on any device today.
- Even if it were deployed, the policy could not reach a device. The settings download channel has never worked (sync.js:64 ships the literal placeholder '%%CONFIG_URL%%'; _fetchRemoteConfig bails at sync.js:691). The policy, the cloud addresses for the publish door, and the PIN door all arrive through that channel.
- No policy has ever been published anywhere — not even on staging. AZURE-CHUNK-AA-STAGING-LEDGER.md states 'No policy exists in staging AppConfig yet, so even the applied LAs behave exactly as before'. So 'code-complete and triple-audited clean' and 'never once run for real' are both true at the same time.
- Only ONE of the eight cloud doors actually consults the policy: the door that saves the policy itself. The two doors every stock movement passes through, plus cost prices, archived history, user management, catalogue publishing and the PIN-pass minting, are all unchanged in the cloud (staging ledger items 5-11: 'simply not done').
- The master enforcement switch does not exist as code. It was DECIDED on 2026-07-30 (one AppConfig row, access_policy_enforce, arming every door together) and then PARKED after three audit rounds produced three BLOCKs. Grep confirms zero references outside markdown. The client-side early-warning flag (_policyRequired) doesn't exist either. Today 'activation' is simply policy-presence.
- The Director's Stock Take PIN panel is half-converted. Setting and clearing the PIN correctly route through the policy under activation (index.html:5877-5898), but the panel that displays the status still reads only the OLD local PIN (index.html:5851-5869). Under an active policy it would show 'No PIN Set' and hide the Clear PIN button even though a policy PIN is live. The helper that fixes it (_pinSource, index.html:5752) already exists and is used on the two staff-facing PIN screens.
- The 'Ask for my password again when…' list offers 10 toggles but the app only honours 3 of them. Publish, user management, backup and access-policy ALWAYS prompt no matter what the toggle says (index.html:4234 never consults the map); archive, stock adjustments and cancel transfers never prompt at all, because the app never mints those proof types. The cloud WOULD honour all 10 — so client and cloud would disagree about what a Director gets asked.
- 'See selling prices' is a curtain, not a lock, and the code says so out loud (index.html:3413, 3884). The selling price is still sent to every device in the shared product list; the toggle only hides columns and export columns. Same for 'view profit reports', 'view transfer history' and 'store-comparison charts'. Only 'see cost prices' and 'see older data' are genuinely enforceable — and neither of those cloud doors is applied.
- Activating the policy is a real, visible behaviour change for staff on day one: the plain store account can no longer receive a Head Office transfer without entering the 24-hour PIN, and once the policy is on, entering that PIN requires internet at that moment (the on-device PIN check is replaced by a cloud check). The screen banner does warn about this in plain English (index.html:5111).
- 'Clear PIN' was built as an INSTANT kill switch across all devices, not wait-for-expiry — which settles AA-20, which is DECIDED and built. Five explicit test cases for it passed when I ran test/access-policy-proof.js today.
- The written enforcement matrix disagrees with the code in two places. It still lists a 'seeCharts' capability (row 19) that was deliberately dropped from the code as unenforceable, and it describes a store_eras archive cutoff (row 18 / §1) that exists in no source file at all. Where they disagree, the code is right and the document is stale.
- A comment inside the cloud rule engine still says per-account exceptions are keyed by user id (accessPolicy.js:41, 'override keys = UserId'), but the code on both sides keys them by username — that was audit fix AA-01. Comment drift only, but it is exactly the kind of thing that caused the original bug (exceptions that silently did nothing).

that is glossed over is a defect waiting to happen.

**Sync, offline and backup**

- Whether a PRODUCTION config Logic App exists at all. The docs name bob-stock-config-staging and the changes made to it, but I found no committed record of a live bob-stock-config resource or its address. To settle it: `az logic workflow list` (or the portal) across the production resource group, plus whether any secret/variable holding the config URL was ever created for the deploy. Marked appliedToCloud 'partial' for the config channel because of this.
- Whether the LIVE push-v2 / pull-v2 Logic Apps are the plain originals or have had the Chunk-2 honest-receipt validation ported across. AZURE-STAGING-ENVIRONMENT.md:26 describes the intended flow ('port the proven change to live') but no doc records it actually happening. It does not affect switchedOn (the client cannot reach them) but it changes what happens on the day sync is turned on. To settle it: export the live workflow definitions and compare against the staging validate variants.
- Whether the un-pushed working branch would actually deploy cleanly. staticwebapp.config.json is modified in the working tree (adding the /data/* block) and test/check-publish-safety.js is untracked, so the intended cutover state is not yet committed. I did not run any gate, so I cannot confirm the branch is release-ready — only that the code paths exist.
- Real-device behaviour of the offline path. Everything here is read from source plus the recorded sentinel names; I did not run the browser suite (instructed not to run smoke-test.js). The specific claims I could not observe first-hand: that the red offline bar actually appears on a real tablet, that the install banner behaves as described, and that a full backup/restore round-trip completes on a device with a realistic amount of data. To settle it: a single manual pass on a real store tablet — go offline, record a movement, close and reopen the app, come back online.
- Whether navigator.storage.persist() is actually being granted. The app fires it and ignores the answer (index.html:6339). On iOS in particular a refusal means the local database can be silently evicted, which for an offline-first app is a data-loss path with no warning. To settle it: log the boolean it resolves to on each target device type.

**The local database and data safety**

- Whether the LIVE push endpoint already returns per-row rejections. The client-side parking of refused rows is written and tested, but AZURE-CHUNK-AA-LA-CHANGES.md §3 lists push-v2 ingest validation as still-to-apply on STAGING, while §119 talks about 'the live push-v2' already having reject machinery. To settle it: send one deliberately malformed row to the production push Logic App with a read-only test credential and look at the response body.

To settle it: load the current live app, create some movements, then point the same browser profile at the branch build and confirm the data is intact and the new table appears.
- How much data actually fits before the restore path breaks. Restore routes the whole database through a single simple-storage key with a browser limit around 5MB, while the database itself can hold far more. To settle it: generate a realistic year of movements for 9 stores, export, and try to restore it.
- Whether the browser actually grants the 'keep my storage' request on the store devices (Windows PCs, iPhones, Androids). The call is fire-and-forget and the answer is thrown away. To settle it: log the result of navigator.storage.persist() and check navigator.storage.persisted() on one device of each type.
- Whether the sync web addresses really are lost on every tab close in the live build, which would mean devices are offline islands most of the time. I read the code (session storage only) but did not run the live app. To settle it: open the live site, enter sync settings, close the tab, reopen, and see whether sync still works.

**Archiving and long-term data**

- Whether the 'Load archived data' path can add phantom stock. Archived rows carry the original cloud row number so they are normally excluded from the stock count — but if that number is missing, the row is treated as recent and WOULD be added to on-screen stock, which a stock take then reads as its system count. To settle it: run the archive read against real archived rows and check whether SourceId is always populated, then re-run test/smoke-test.js S-207 with a SourceId-less row.
- Whether the archive run's lock is reliably released when the cloud errors mid-copy (as opposed to the run refusing cleanly). It did stick on 'running' during the July work. To settle it: force an insert failure on a staging probe list and inspect the archive_state row afterwards.
- Whether the 5,000-row-per-run ceiling has ever been exercised. The refusal path is documented but has no test. To settle it: seed a staging probe list with 5,001 rows and confirm the run returns 413 and changes nothing.
- Whether the 'archive' permission gate on the run itself honours the newer permission model. AZURE-CHUNK-AA-LA-CHANGES.md §10 says 'no structural change', and item 11 of AZURE-CHUNK-AA-STAGING-LEDGER.md still lists it as not applied. To settle it: read the Call_verify / Check_proof actions in the deployed archive definition against validateUser.js.
- Whether the archived-transfer-reference list stored on each device has a size ceiling. It is bounded to references named by transfers/deliveries/stock takes, which should stay small, but nothing caps or prunes it (db.js:559-578). To settle it: model the reference count at a realistic multi-store volume over several years.
